[ { "tool_use_id": "toolu_015itixraq34qdof25Tu669V", "type": "tool_result", "content": "1\t# Shot Charts (BallR) — Feasibility Investigation\n2\t\n3\tStatus: investigation only — nothing implemented.\n4\tAuthor: Sanele (via Claude Code)\n5\tDate: 2026-08-29\n6\tRepo under review: https://github.com/toddwschneider/ballr (MIT, © 2016 Todd Schneider)\n7\t\n8\t## 1. What we want\n9\t\n10\tAdd a shot chart to the player profile page — a half-court diagram showing\n11\twhere a player took their shots, makes vs misses, and shooting efficiency by\n12\tzone (typically as a hexbin map coloured by FG% relative to league average).\n13\t\n14\t## 2. What BallR actually is\n15\t\n16\tBallR is a self-contained R / Shiny web app (~15 .R files, no build, no\n17\tdatabase). It is not a library you embed — it's a standalone interactive\n18\tdashboard. Its value to us is not the code as a dependency but as a\n19\treference implementation of two things:\n20\t\n21\t1. How to get shot data out of the NBA Stats API.\n22\t2. The court geometry + hexbin math to turn (x, y, made?) shot rows into a\n23\t   good-looking chart.\n24\t\n25\t### 2.1 File map\n26\t\n27\t| File | Role | Relevance to us |\n28\t|---|---|---|\n29\t| fetch_shots.R | Calls stats.nba.com/stats/shotchartdetail, parses the response | High — the ingestion recipe |\n30\t| players_data.R | Calls commonallplayers, defines HTTP request_headers | Medium — header recipe (we already solve this in throttle.py) |\n31\t| plot_court.R | Draws the half-court in feet, (0,0) = hoop | High — court geometry to port to SVG |\n32\t| hex_chart.R | calculate_hexbins_from_shots() — bins shots, joins league averages, computes FG%-vs-league diff | High — the binning + colour algorithm |\n33\t| heatmap_chart.R | KDE heat map variant | Optional |\n34\t| scatter_chart.R | Plain made/missed dot plot | Low effort, good v1 |\n35\t| court_themes.R | Colour palettes for the court | Cosmetic reference |\n36\t| helpers.R | Formatters, short_three_seasons (1994–97 had a shorter 3pt line) | Minor edge case |\n37\t| server.R / ui.R | Shiny glue | Not relevant — this is the part we don't reuse |\n38\t| heatmap_chart.R, lebron.R | Extra charts / example script | Optional |\n39\t\n40\t### 2.2 Tech stack (theirs)\n41\t\n42\tR, Shiny, tidyverse, hexbin, ggplot2 >= 3.0, httr. None of this fits\n43\tour stack (NestJS + Prisma + Python ingestion + React/Recharts). We would not\n44\trun any R.\n45\t\n46\t## 3. The data source — shotchartdetail\n47\t\n48\tfetch_shots.R verbatim call:\n49\t\n50\t\n51\tGET https://stats.nba.com/stats/shotchartdetail\n52\t  PlayerID       = <nba player id>\n53\t  Season         = \"2025-26\"\n54\t  SeasonType     = \"Regular Season\"   # or \"Playoffs\"\n55\t  ContextMeasure = \"FGA\"\n56\t  LeagueID       = \"00\"\n57\t  TeamID = 0, GameID = \"\", Period = 0, LastNGames = 0, Month = 0,\n58\t  OpponentTeamID = 0, + ~12 more empty/zero filter params\n59\t  (headers: browser User-Agent + Accept/Referer — same cloud-IP-blocked\n60\t   endpoint we already deal with in apps/ingestion)\n61\t\n62\t\n63\tResponse: two result sets.\n64\t\n65\t- resultSets[0] — one row per shot for that player/season. Key columns:\n66\t  LOC_X, LOC_Y (tenths of a foot; origin at hoop), SHOT_DISTANCE,\n67\t  SHOT_MADE_FLAG (1/0), SHOT_TYPE ("2PT"/"3PT Field Goal"),\n68\t  SHOT_ZONE_BASIC, SHOT_ZONE_AREA, SHOT_ZONE_RANGE, ACTION_TYPE\n69\t  ("Jump Shot", "Layup", …), GAME_ID, GAME_DATE, PERIOD.\n70\t- resultSets[1] — league averages for that season, one row per\n71\t  SHOT_ZONE_BASIC × SHOT_ZONE_AREA × SHOT_ZONE_RANGE: FGA, FGM,\n72\t  FG_PCT. This is what makes the hexbin "hot/cold vs league" colouring\n73\t  possible without a second request.\n74\t\n75\tCoordinate transform BallR applies (fetch_shots.R):\n76\t\n77\t\n78\tloc_x =  -LOC_X / 10          # feet, flipped so left/right matches a viewer\n79\tloc_y =   LOC_Y / 10 + 5.25   # feet, 5.25 = hoop_center_y\n80\tshot_value = 3 if SHOT_TYPE contains \"3pt\" else 2\n81\t\n82\t\n83\t### 3.1 Good news for us\n84\t\n85\t- shotchartdetail is exactly the same API family (stats.nba.com/stats/*)\n86\t  we already ingest from in apps/ingestion. Same headers problem, same\n87\t  rate-limit discipline (throttle.py), same cloud-IP block (must run from a\n88\t  residential network — apps/ingestion/README.md). We have all of that\n89\t  solved already.\n90\t- It's one call per player per season (the league-average set comes free in\n91\t  the same response). For a full-league ingest that's ~1 call per ingested\n92\t  player — same order of magnitude as the existing player_bios.py phase\n93\t  (~450–500 calls), so it roughly doubles that phase or adds a comparable one.\n94\t- nba_api (the Python package we already use) has a\n95\t  nba_api.stats.endpoints.shotchartdetail.ShotChartDetail wrapper — we would\n96\t  not hand-roll the HTTP the way BallR does; we'd add it to\n97\t  apps/ingestion the same way games.py uses boxscoretraditionalv3.\n98\t\n99\t### 3.2 Concerns\n100\t\n101\t- Volume: a high-usage player takes ~1,500 shots/season. League-wide that's\n102\t  roughly 200k–250k shot rows per season. That's a real table — needs an\n103\t  index on playerId (+ season, seasonType if we add postseason) and we\n104\t  should not ship it to the browser raw; the API should return either\n105\t  pre-binned hexes or a capped/simplified point set.\n106\t- Play-by-play vs shotchart: shotchartdetail is independent of our\n107\t  GameEvent / PlayerGameStat derivation chain. It is a new external data\n108\t  feed, not something derived from events we hold. Worth noting against the\n109\t  brief's "stats trace back to event records" framing — shot charts are\n110\t  descriptive raw data, arguably fine, but the team should be deliberate about\n111\t  it. Could also be pitched as the "second external API integration" the\n112\t  brief wants (README "What's not done yet") — though it's the same host as the\n113\t  first, so that may not satisfy the requirement.\n114\t- Coordinate origin/scale is NBA-specific tribal knowledge (tenths of a\n115\t  foot, hoop at y≈5.25 ft, half-court ~47 ft, 3pt arc r=23.75 ft, corner 3 at\n116\t  x=±22 ft). plot_court.R is the cleanest reference for these numbers.\n117\t- The short 3pt line seasons (1994-97) don't matter to us — we're on\n118\t  2025-26 only.\n119\t\n120\t## 4. How it fits into our project\n121\t\n122\tWe reuse the approach, not the code. Concretely:\n123\t\n124\t### 4.1 Ingestion (apps/ingestion) — new phase\n125\t\n126\t- New shots.py: fetch_player_shots(nba_player_id, season, season_type) via\n127\t  nba_api's ShotChartDetail, throttled through call_with_rate_limit.\n128\t- New phase in ingest.py after boxscores: loop ingested players, upsert shot\n129\t  rows. Idempotent upsert keyed on a natural key (nbaPlayerId + gameId +\n130\t  GAME_EVENT_ID, which shotchartdetail provides).\n131\t- Optionally cache the per-season league averages result set in its own\n132\t  small table (one refresh per season) so the API can compute\n133\t  FG%-vs-league without recomputing from every shot.\n134\t\n135\t### 4.2 Schema (apps/api/prisma) — new model\n136\t\n137\tprisma\n138\tmodel Shot {\n139\t  id            String   @id @default(uuid())\n140\t  playerId      String\n141\t  player        Player   @relation(fields: [playerId], references: [id])\n142\t  gameId        String?\n143\t  game          Game?    @relation(fields: [gameId], references: [id])\n144\t  season        String\n145\t  seasonType    String   // \"REGULAR\" | \"PLAYOFFS\" (ties into the postseason plan)\n146\t  locX          Int      // raw API tenths-of-foot, transform at read time\n147\t  locY          Int\n148\t  shotDistance  Int\n149\t  made          Boolean\n150\t  shotValue     Int      // 2 or 3\n151\t  zoneBasic     String\n152\t  zoneArea      String\n153\t  zoneRange     String\n154\t  actionType    String\n155\t  period        Int\n156\t  gameDate      DateTime\n157\t\n158\t  @@index([playerId, season, seasonType])\n159\t  @@unique([playerId, gameId, /* gameEventId */ ])\n160\t}\n161\t\n162\t\n163\tOptionally a LeagueShotZoneAverage model (season, zoneBasic, zoneArea,\n164\tzoneRange, fga, fgm, fgPct).\n165\t\n166\t### 4.3 API (apps/api/src) — new endpoint\n167\t\n168\tGET /v1/players/:id/shots?season=&seasonType=&format=hex|scatter\n169\t\n170\t- Server does the hexbin aggregation (port hex_chart.R's\n171\t  calculate_hexbins_from_shots logic to TypeScript — it's ~50 lines of\n172\t  grid-bin + group-by + join-to-league-average + clamp) so the browser gets a\n173\t  few hundred hex cells, not 1,500 raw points.\n174\t- Returns { hexes: [{ x, y, attempts, fgPct, leagueFgPct, pointsPerShot }],\n175\t  courtDimensions } or, for scatter mode, a capped point list.\n176\t- Ties into the postseason view plan — seasonType is the same\n177\t  discriminator; a Finals shot chart would just be another segment.\n178\t\n179\t### 4.4 Frontend (apps/web) — new component\n180\t\n181\t- ShotChart.tsx on PlayerProfilePage, below the existing "Shooting splits"\n182\t  card. We already have CourtView.tsx (used on the game detail page) — check\n183\t  whether its court SVG can be extended rather than drawing a second court.\n184\t- Render as inline SVG (hand-drawn court from plot_court.R's geometry +\n185\t  <polygon> hexes coloured on a diverging scale) — Recharts has no native\n186\t  hexbin, and d3-hexbin would be a new dependency for one chart. SVG hexes off\n187\t  a server-computed grid is the least-dependency path.\n188\t- Court lines: port the exact measurements from plot_court.R (documented in §3\n189\t  above).\n190\t- Colour scale: red→grey→green diverging on fgPct - leagueFgPct, clamped to\n191\t  ±~10% like BallR's bounded values. Must pass the same WCAG contrast bar the\n192\t  rest of the theme holds to (PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md "Theme").\n193\t\n194\t### 4.5 What we copy vs. write\n195\t\n196\t| Thing | Source | Action |\n197\t|---|---|---|\n198\t| shotchartdetail params + response shape | fetch_shots.R | Copy as spec; call via nba_api |\n199\t| Coordinate transform (/10, hoop y offset, x flip) | fetch_shots.R | Copy the constants |\n200\t| Court geometry (feet measurements) | plot_court.R | Copy the numbers into an SVG |\n201\t| Hexbin grid + league-avg join + clamp | hex_chart.R | Re-implement in TS (~50–80 lines) |\n202\t| KDE heat map | heatmap_chart.R | Skip for v1 |\n203\t| Shiny UI, R plumbing, court_themes.R | server.R/ui.R | Ignore entirely |\n204\t\n205\tNo R runs anywhere. No BallR code is imported. MIT license means we could\n206\tcopy snippets with attribution, but we'd be translating algorithms, not\n207\tvendoring files — a short credit in docs/ai-usage.md / a code comment\n208\tpointing at the repo is the right level of attribution.\n209\t\n210\t## 5. Effort estimate\n211\t\n212\t| Piece | Size |\n213\t|---|---|\n214\t| apps/ingestion shot phase + throttle reuse | ~0.5 day |\n215\t| Prisma Shot model + migration + backfill considerations | ~0.5 day |\n216\t| API endpoint + hexbin port + tests | ~1–1.5 days |\n217\t| ShotChart.tsx SVG component + court + colour scale + tests | ~1.5–2 days |\n218\t| Seed fixtures so local/e2e has shots | ~0.25 day |\n219\t| Total | ~4–5 days |\n220\t\n221\tBiggest unknowns: (a) the SVG court + hex rendering polish, (b) table size /\n222\tquery performance at league scale, (c) whether CourtView.tsx can be reused.\n223\t\n224\t## 6. Risks & open questions\n225\t\n226\t| Risk | Note |\n227\t|---|---|\n228\t| stats.nba.com cloud-IP block | Already handled in apps/ingestion — ingestion must run from a residential network. No new problem. |\n229\t| Shot table size (~200k rows/season) | Needs proper indexing; API must return aggregated hexes, never raw rows, to the browser. |\n230\t| "Second external API" brief requirement | Same host as existing ingestion — may not count as a second integration. Confirm with the team. |\n231\t| Event-sourcing framing | Shot data is a new raw feed, not derived from our GameEvent rows. Team should decide if that's acceptable or if it should be framed as descriptive-only. |\n232\t| No hexbin in Recharts | Use inline SVG off server-computed bins; avoid adding d3-hexbin. |\n233\t| Court coordinate correctness | Port exact constants from plot_court.R; add a visual snapshot test. |\n234\t| nba_api ShotChartDetail wrapper drift | Verify the live response shape during implementation, same as games.py did for BoxScoreTraditionalV3. |\n235\t| Historical seasons / short 3pt line | Out of scope — 2025-26 only. |\n236\t\n237\t## 7. Recommendation\n238\t\n239\tWorth doing, as a follow-up after the postseason-view work (they share the\n240\tseasonType discriminator and both touch PlayerProfilePage). Sequence:\n241\t\n242\t1. Prototype the ingestion call for one player via nba_api and inspect the\n243\t   real response.\n244\t2. Build the Shot model + a scatter chart first (individual made/missed dots) —\n245\t   low math, proves the court SVG and the data path.\n246\t3. Add the hexbin aggregation + league-average colouring as a second pass.\n247\t4. Heat map only if there's demand.\n248\t\n249\tDo not adopt BallR as a dependency or run any R — treat it purely as a\n250\tdocumented reference for the API params, coordinate system, and binning\n251\talgorithm.\n252\t\n253\t" } ]

[ { "tool_use_id": "toolu_011Ph5Chjfs39qb53pjjyowm", "type": "tool_result", "content": "1\t# Player Similarity & Archetypes — Feasibility Investigation\n2\t\n3\tStatus: investigation only — nothing implemented.\n4\tAuthor: Sanele (via Claude Code)\n5\tDate: 2026-08-29\n6\t\n7\t## 1. Goal\n8\t\n9\tTwo linked features on the player profile:\n10\t\n11\t1. Archetype — a clustering model groups every player into a playing-style\n12\t   bucket ("Primary Ball Handler", "3&D Wing", "Slasher", "Stretch Big",\n13\t   "Rim-Running Big", "Bench Scorer", …). Show the label on the profile.\n14\t2. Similar players — for a selected player, show the 5–6 most stylistically\n15\t   similar players, each linking to their profile.\n16\t\n17\t## 2. Verdict\n18\t\n19\tFeasible for a credible v1, with honest caveats — and much stronger if we add\n20\tone cheap extra nba_api call.\n21\t\n22\t- The pattern already exists in the repo: apps/predictor and apps/optimizer\n23\t  are small Python services that read PlayerGameStat, compute something, and\n24\t  write results to their own tables that NestJS only reads. A\n25\t  apps/similarity service is the same shape.\n26\t- The similar-players half is low-risk — it's a nearest-neighbour lookup in\n27\t  a standardized feature space and doesn't even require the clustering to work\n28\t  well.\n29\t- The archetype half is the harder, more subjective half: clustering gives\n30\t  you unlabeled groups, and someone has to map centroids to names. Cluster\n31\t  identity is also not stable across re-runs without extra work (§6.4).\n32\t- Data sufficiency: box-score data alone gives a usable but blunt\n33\t  feature set (§4). The honest framing — like apps/predictor/README.md's\n34\t  "read before trusting the numbers" — is that this is a box-score style\n35\t  archetype model, not the tracking-data archetype systems (BBall-Index\n36\t  LEBRON, etc.) it superficially resembles.\n37\t\n38\t## 3. What data we have\n39\t\n40\t### 3.1 In the database today\n41\t\n42\tPlayerGameStat (per player per game): minutes, points, rebounds,\n43\tassists, steals, blocks, turnovers, fieldGoalsMade/Attempted,\n44\tthreesMade/Attempted, freeThrowsMade/Attempted.\n45\t\n46\tPlayer: position (string, often \"N/A\" from the roster feed),\n47\theightInches, weightLbs, plus bio (draftYear, seasonExp, …).\n48\t\n49\tGame: gameDate, season.\n50\t\n51\t### 3.2 Volume\n52\t\n53\t- ~450–500 players ingested (full league rosters).\n54\t- GAMES_PER_TEAM = 15 in apps/ingestion/games.py — so each player has\n55\t  at most ~15 game rows, and bench players far fewer. Rotation players get\n56\t  a decent sample; deep-bench players are mostly noise.\n57\t- Regular season only today (postseason is a separate plan).\n58\t\n59\t### 3.3 What's missing for "real" archetype work\n60\t\n61\tPlayerGameStat has no: usage rate, assist %, rebound % (or the\n62\toffensive/defensive rebound split), true shooting %, on/off data, shot\n63\tlocation/zone, touches, drives, passes, time of possession, defensive matchup\n64\tdata, screen assists, or shot-type breakdown. Real positional-archetype models\n65\tlean heavily on exactly these.\n66\t\n67\t### 3.4 The cheap upgrade: leaguedashplayerstats\n68\t\n69\tnba_api's leaguedashplayerstats.LeagueDashPlayerStats with\n70\tmeasure_type_detailed_defense=\"Advanced\" returns, in one league-wide call,\n71\tper-player season aggregates including USG_PCT, AST_PCT, AST_TO,\n72\tAST_RATIO, OREB_PCT, DREB_PCT, REB_PCT, TS_PCT, EFG_PCT, PACE.\n73\tA second call with measure_type=\"Base\" and per_mode=\"Per100Possessions\"\n74\tgives per-100 counting stats. measure_type=\"Scoring\" gives\n75\tPCT_PTS_2PT_MR, PCT_PTS_FB, PCT_PTS_PAINT, PCT_AST_FGM,\n76\tPCT_UAST_FGM — shot-diet features that are genuinely archetype-defining.\n77\t\n78\tStrong recommendation: ingest these 2–3 calls into a new\n79\tPlayerSeasonAdvanced table. It's ~3 API calls total (not per-player), reuses\n80\tthrottle.py, and turns "blunt box-score model" into "reasonable\n81\tstyle model". Everything below works with or without it, but is markedly better\n82\twith it.\n83\t\n84\t## 4. Feature engineering\n85\t\n86\tAll features are rate stats, standardized (z-score) before modelling —\n87\tclustering and distance metrics are meaningless on raw mixed-scale columns\n88\t(points ~20, FG% ~0.45, height ~78). Aggregate each player's game rows to\n89\tseason totals first, then derive rates.\n90\t\n91\t### 4.1 From box-score data only (available now)\n92\t\n93\t| Feature | Formula | Captures |\n94\t|---|---|---|\n95\t| Points / 36 | pts * 36 / min | scoring volume |\n96\t| Rebounds / 36 | reb * 36 / min | rebounding |\n97\t| Assists / 36 | ast * 36 / min | playmaking volume |\n98\t| Steals / 36, Blocks / 36 | * 36 / min | defensive events / rim protection |\n99\t| Turnovers / 36 | * 36 / min | ball-handling load |\n100\t| 3PA rate | 3PA / FGA | floor spacing |\n101\t| FT rate | FTA / FGA | rim pressure / drawing fouls |\n102\t| eFG% | (FGM + 0.5*3PM) / FGA | shooting efficiency |\n103\t| TS% | pts / (2*(FGA + 0.44*FTA)) | scoring efficiency |\n104\t| Assist-to-turnover | ast / tov | shot creation quality |\n105\t| Shot volume / min | (FGA + 0.44*FTA + TOV) / min | usage proxy (no team possessions, so approximate) |\n106\t| Height, weight | Player | size |\n107\t| 3PA / 36 | 3PA * 36 / min | shooter vs non-shooter |\n108\t\n109\t~13 features. Enough to separate guards from bigs, shooters from slashers,\n110\thigh-usage creators from low-usage roleplayers.\n111\t\n112\t### 4.2 With PlayerSeasonAdvanced added (recommended)\n113\t\n114\tAdd/replace proxies with the real thing: USG_PCT, AST_PCT, AST_TO,\n115\tOREB_PCT, DREB_PCT, TS_PCT, and the Scoring shot-diet percentages\n116\t(PCT_PTS_PAINT, PCT_PTS_MR, PCT_PTS_3PT, PCT_UAST_FGM). This is what\n117\tlets the model distinguish e.g. a "Primary Ball Handler" (high USG, high AST%,\n118\thigh unassisted-FG%) from a "Movement Shooter" (low USG, low AST%, high\n119\tassisted-3PT%).\n120\t\n121\t### 4.3 Eligibility filter\n122\t\n123\tOnly cluster / rank players above a minutes floor — e.g. ≥ 8 games AND ≥ 12\n124\tMPG (tune against the ingested data). Everyone else gets no archetype\n125\t("Not enough minutes") rather than a garbage one. This is the single most\n126\timportant lever on output quality given the 15-game sample.\n127\t\n128\t## 5. Suitable models\n129\t\n130\t### 5.1 Archetype (clustering)\n131\t\n132\t| Model | Fit here | Notes |\n133\t|---|---|---|\n134\t| K-Means | Recommended baseline | Fast, deterministic with fixed random_state + k-means++. Hard assignment. Pick k via silhouette score, constrained to an interpretable 6–9. Assumes roughly spherical clusters in the standardized space — acceptable. |\n135\t| Gaussian Mixture (GMM) | Recommended for the UI | Soft membership → "68% Slasher / 22% Ball Handler / 10% Wing", which is a nicer profile display and more honest about tweeners. Slightly less stable; needs the same k choice. |\n136\t| Agglomerative / hierarchical | Good secondary | No need to pre-commit to k; produces a dendrogram (could power a "style tree" viz). O(n²) is fine at n≈450. |\n137\t| DBSCAN / HDBSCAN | Not for the main grouping | Density-based; finds outliers ("no one plays like Jokić") and leaves them unclustered. NBA feature density is uneven, so it tends to dump half the league into one blob. Interesting as an "is this player unique?" flag only. |\n138\t| PCA / UMAP | Not a cluster model | Dimensionality reduction. Use PCA (2–3 components) to (a) de-noise before K-Means and (b) drive a 2D "style map" scatter on the frontend. UMAP looks nicer but adds a heavy dependency and non-determinism. |\n139\t\n140\tRecommended stack: standardize → optional PCA to ~5 dims → K-Means for the\n141\tlabel + GMM for soft membership (both cheap, run both, store both).\n142\t\n143\t### 5.2 Similar players (nearest neighbours)\n144\t\n145\tIndependent of clustering. In the standardized (or PCA) feature space, compute\n146\teach player's 6 nearest neighbours by Euclidean distance (cosine is an\n147\toption but Euclidean on z-scored features is the standard choice here).\n148\tsklearn.neighbors.NearestNeighbors, or just a pairwise distance matrix —\n149\t450×450 is trivial. Convert distance to a 0–100 "similarity score" for display\n150\tvia 100 * exp(-distance / scale) or 100 * (1 - dist/max_dist).\n151\t\n152\tOptionally constrain: "similar players at the same position" as a UI toggle.\n153\t\n154\t### 5.3 Archetype naming\n155\t\n156\tClustering will not name the groups. Process:\n157\t\n158\t1. Fit the model, print each cluster's centroid in original (un-standardized)\n159\t   units + the 5 most representative players (closest to centroid).\n160\t2. A human maps each cluster to a name and stores the mapping in a small\n161\t   committed file (apps/similarity/archetype_labels.py or a DB table).\n162\t3. On re-fit, align new clusters to old labels by matching centroids (§6.4).\n163\t\n164\tExpect to iterate on k and the labels a few times before it "reads right".\n165\t\n166\t## 6. Risks & failure modes\n167\t\n168\t| Risk | Mitigation |\n169\t|---|---|\n170\t| 15 games/player is a thin sample — rate stats for low-minute players are noisy | Minutes/games eligibility filter (§4.3); consider raising GAMES_PER_TEAM or doing a season-wide leaguedashplayerstats pull that already aggregates the full season |\n171\t| Box-score features are blunt — can't tell a 3&D wing from a low-usage off-ball scorer | Add PlayerSeasonAdvanced (§3.4); document the limitation honestly |\n172\t| Cluster labels not stable across re-runs — K-Means cluster 0 today ≠ cluster 0 tomorrow | Fix random_state; after re-fit, match each new centroid to the nearest old centroid and carry the label forward; store a reference centroid set |\n173\t| k is subjective | Silhouette/elbow to narrow, then pick for interpretability; treat k as a tuned constant like MINIMUM_GAMES_FOR_REGRESSION in four_factors.py |\n174\t| Position field is unreliable (\"N/A\" from the roster feed) | Don't feed raw position as a feature; derive style from stats + size. Position is display-only |\n175\t| Tweeners get a misleading hard label | Show GMM soft membership, or show "primary / secondary" archetype |\n176\t| Users read "similar" as "as good as" | Label it "similar style", show the similarity score, don't rank by quality |\n177\t| Small league sample → some archetypes have 3–4 players | Merge tiny clusters; cap k |\n178\t| Determinism in CI/tests | All randomness seeded; test asserts cluster sizes / a known player's archetype stay put |\n179\t\n180\t## 7. How it fits the project\n181\t\n182\t### 7.1 New Python service — apps/similarity/\n183\t\n184\tMirrors apps/predictor / apps/optimizer exactly:\n185\t\n186\t\n187\tapps/similarity/\n188\t  db.py               # same psycopg2 connection helper as the others\n189\t  features.py         # PlayerGameStat (+ PlayerSeasonAdvanced) -> feature matrix\n190\t  cluster.py          # standardize -> PCA -> KMeans + GMM -> labels\n191\t  similarity.py       # NearestNeighbors -> top-6 per player\n192\t  archetype_labels.py # committed centroid->name mapping\n193\t  build_archetypes.py # orchestrator: fit, align labels, write tables\n194\t  requirements.txt    # + scikit-learn, numpy (pandas optional)\n195\t  README.md           # honest-limitations section like predictor/README.md\n196\t\n197\t\n198\tNew dependency: scikit-learn (numpy already used by apps/predictor).\n199\tRun cadence: re-run after each ingestion, same as predict_games.py /\n200\tpredict.py (documented in apps/ingestion/README.md's "re-run downstream\n201\tservices" list).\n202\t\n203\t### 7.2 Schema (apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma)\n204\t\n205\tprisma\n206\tmodel PlayerArchetype {\n207\t  id            String   @id @default(uuid())\n208\t  playerId      String   @unique\n209\t  player        Player   @relation(fields: [playerId], references: [id])\n210\t  season        String\n211\t  primaryLabel  String            // \"3&D Wing\"\n212\t  clusterId     Int\n213\t  membership    Json              // { \"3&D Wing\": 0.68, \"Slasher\": 0.22, ... } from GMM\n214\t  featureVector Json              // standardized features, for the radar/plot\n215\t  distanceToCentroid Float\n216\t  computedAt    DateTime @default(now())\n217\t  @@index([playerId])\n218\t}\n219\t\n220\tmodel PlayerSimilarity {\n221\t  id              String  @id @default(uuid())\n222\t  playerId        String\n223\t  player          Player  @relation(\"SimilarFrom\", fields: [playerId], references: [id])\n224\t  similarPlayerId String\n225\t  similarPlayer   Player  @relation(\"SimilarTo\", fields: [similarPlayerId], references: [id])\n226\t  rank            Int              // 1..6\n227\t  similarityScore Float            // 0..100\n228\t  @@unique([playerId, similarPlayerId])\n229\t  @@index([playerId, rank])\n230\t}\n231\t\n232\t\n233\tOptionally PlayerSeasonAdvanced (§3.4). Standard additive migration; the\n234\tPython service is the only writer, NestJS reads only — same contract the schema\n235\tcomments already describe for PlayerPrediction / GamePrediction.\n236\t\n237\tPrecompute-and-store (rather than live kNN in the API) matches the existing\n238\tPlayerPrediction pattern and keeps API reads a plain indexed lookup.\n239\t\n240\t### 7.3 API (apps/api/src/players/)\n241\t\n242\tGET /v1/players/:id/archetype (public, like /stats):\n243\t\n244\tjsonc\n245\t{\n246\t  \"playerId\": \"…\",\n247\t  \"archetype\": { \"primaryLabel\": \"3&D Wing\", \"membership\": { … }, \"clusterId\": 4 },\n248\t  \"similarPlayers\": [\n249\t    { \"player\": { …PlayerWithTeam }, \"rank\": 1, \"similarityScore\": 91.4 },\n250\t    …\n251\t  ]\n252\t}\n253\t\n254\t\n255\t- New ArchetypeService reading PlayerArchetype + PlayerSimilarity\n256\t  (join similarPlayer.team for the cards).\n257\t- Two-step 404 like PlayersController.getPlayerStats: player-not-found vs.\n258\t  player-found-but-not-enough-minutes-for-an-archetype.\n259\t- Optional GET /v1/archetypes → list of archetype labels + member counts, for\n260\t  a browse page.\n261\t\n262\t### 7.4 Frontend (apps/web)\n263\t\n264\t- types/nba.ts: PlayerArchetypeResponse, SimilarPlayer.\n265\t- nbaApi.ts: fetchPlayerArchetype(playerId).\n266\t- PlayerProfilePage.tsx: new card "Style & similar players" below the Bio\n267\t  card —\n268\t  - archetype badge + (if GMM) a small stacked bar of soft membership,\n269\t  - a 5–6 item list of similar players (PlayerHeadshot + name + team badge +\n270\t    similarity score), each a Link to /players/:id,\n271\t  - "Not enough minutes this season" empty state.\n272\t- On the profile card: a small 2D "style map" thumbnail plotting the player\n273\t  among their cluster (a cropped view of the full map in §7.6).\n274\t- The full "View clusters" screen — see §7.6.\n275\t\n276\t### 7.5 Testing\n277\t\n278\t- features.py unit tests: per-36 math, usage proxy, eligibility filter, divide-by-zero (no minutes).\n279\t- cluster.py: with a fixed seed + a small synthetic matrix, assert stable\n280\t  cluster assignment; assert label-alignment logic carries names forward when\n281\t  centroids barely move.\n282\t- similarity.py: nearest-neighbour correctness on a hand-built matrix; a\n283\t  player is never their own neighbour; symmetric-ish results.\n284\t- API: ArchetypeService e2e against the disposable Postgres DB with seeded\n285\t  PlayerArchetype/PlayerSimilarity rows; 404 paths.\n286\t- seed.ts: add archetype + similarity fixtures for the 12 mock players so the\n287\t  card renders locally and in web tests.\n288\t\n289\t### 7.6 Interactive cluster map — the "View clusters" screen\n290\t\n291\tFeasible, and a natural payoff of the work above — the model already\n292\tproduces everything the screen needs; the main additions are storing 2D layout\n293\tcoordinates and one list endpoint.\n294\t\n295\t#### Layout — compute in Python, store on the row\n296\t\n297\tThe screen is a scatter plot of every eligible player, positioned so that\n298\tstylistically similar players sit near each other and coloured by archetype.\n299\tThe 2D coordinates come from the same standardized feature matrix the\n300\tclustering uses:\n301\t\n302\t| Option | Pick? | Notes |\n303\t|---|---|---|\n304\t| PCA → first 2 components | Recommended default | Deterministic, fast, and the axes are semi-interpretable (PC1 ≈ interior/size vs perimeter/creation, PC2 ≈ usage). Reuses the PCA already in cluster.py. |\n305\t| t-SNE / UMAP | Optional second layout | Visually tighter, better-separated blobs. Non-deterministic (seed it), heavier, axes meaningless. Compute once in Python and store — never in the browser. Offer as a "layout: PCA / UMAP" toggle if there's time. |\n306\t\n307\t- Add plotX Float, plotY Float to PlayerArchetype (or a small\n308\t  PlayerMapPosition table if we want multiple layouts).\n309\t- Sign-canonicalisation matters: PCA eigenvector direction is arbitrary, so\n310\t  the map can mirror-flip between re-fits. Force a deterministic sign (e.g. make\n311\t  the largest-magnitude loading on each component positive) — same family of\n312\t  problem as the cluster-label-stability fix in §6.\n313\t- Optionally compute a convex hull (or alpha-shape) per cluster in Python\n314\t  and store the polygon, so the frontend can shade cluster regions without any\n315\t  geometry code.\n316\t\n317\t#### API\n318\t\n319\tGET /v1/archetypes/map (public) — one query, ~450 rows:\n320\t\n321\tjsonc\n322\t{\n323\t  \"players\": [\n324\t    { \"id\": \"…\", \"firstName\": \"…\", \"lastName\": \"…\", \"headshotUrl\": \"…\",\n325\t      \"team\": { \"abbreviation\": \"LAL\", … },\n326\t      \"clusterId\": 4, \"primaryLabel\": \"3&D Wing\",\n327\t      \"plotX\": -1.82, \"plotY\": 0.44, \"membership\": { … } }\n328\t  ],\n329\t  \"archetypes\": [ { \"clusterId\": 4, \"label\": \"3&D Wing\", \"count\": 61,\n330\t                    \"hull\": [[x,y], …] } ],\n331\t  \"axes\": { \"x\": \"Perimeter creation →\", \"y\": \"Usage →\" }  // PCA loadings summarised\n332\t}\n333\t\n334\t\n335\tPayload is ~60–120 KB, fully cacheable (changes only on re-fit). No pagination —\n336\tthe whole point is seeing everyone at once.\n337\t\n338\t#### Frontend\n339\t\n340\t- New route /clusters (or /archetypes) in App.tsx, public, with a sidebar\n341\t  nav entry — matches Players/Teams being public.\n342\t- Chart: start with Recharts ScatterChart — ~450 points with colour-by-\n343\t  cluster, hover tooltip (headshot + name + archetype), and click → navigate to\n344\t  /players/:id is well within what it handles. Cell per point for the\n345\t  cluster colour (reuse the archetype palette from the profile card).\n346\t- Legend = archetype list; click a label to highlight/isolate that cluster\n347\t  (dim the rest).\n348\t- Search box to find and pan/highlight a specific player.\n349\t- Selected player: halo the point, and draw faint connector lines to their\n350\t  6 similar players (from PlayerSimilarity) — visually ties the two features\n351\t  together.\n352\t- Shaded cluster regions from the stored hulls (nice-to-have).\n353\t- Pan/zoom / lasso is where Recharts runs out of road. If we want smooth\n354\t  zoom over 450+ points, that's a canvas renderer (visx + a custom canvas\n355\t  layer, or regl-scatterplot) — a new dependency, defer to a v2. v1 ships\n356\t  with fixed extent + cluster isolation, which is enough.\n357\t- Accessibility / mobile fallback (required, not optional): a scatter of\n358\t  dots is unusable by keyboard and screen readers, and cramped on a phone.\n359\t  Ship an equivalent grouped, filterable table (archetype → players, sortable\n360\t  by the key stats) alongside the plot, toggled by a "chart / table" switch.\n361\t  This also is the /archetypes browse page from the earlier draft, so it's\n362\t  not extra scope — and it's the right call given the open accessibility-audit\n363\t  gap noted in the root README.md.\n364\t\n365\t#### Effort\n366\t\n367\t~1.5–2.5 days on top of the base feature: plotX/plotY (+ optional hull) in\n368\tcluster.py, the /archetypes/map endpoint + service + test, and the\n369\t/clusters page with the scatter, legend-filter, search, similar-player\n370\tconnectors, and the table fallback.\n371\t\n372\t## 8. Effort estimate\n373\t\n374\t| Piece | Size |\n375\t|---|---|\n376\t| (Recommended) PlayerSeasonAdvanced ingestion — 2–3 nba_api calls | ~0.5 day |\n377\t| apps/similarity service (features + KMeans/GMM + kNN + label alignment) | ~2–2.5 days |\n378\t| Archetype label iteration (fit, inspect, name, repeat) | ~0.5–1 day, mostly judgement |\n379\t| Prisma models + migration + seed fixtures | ~0.5 day |\n380\t| API endpoint + service + tests | ~1 day |\n381\t| Frontend card + types + client + tests | ~1.5 days |\n382\t| Interactive "View clusters" screen (§7.6): layout coords + /archetypes/map endpoint + /clusters page + table fallback | ~1.5–2.5 days |\n383\t| Docs (PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md, service README, ingestion README) | ~0.25 day |\n384\t| Total | ~8–9 days with the cluster screen (~6–7 without it; ~5.5 also dropping the advanced-stats ingestion) |\n385\t\n386\t## 9. Recommendation\n387\t\n388\tWorth doing, and a good showcase feature. Sequence:\n389\t\n390\t1. First add PlayerSeasonAdvanced ingestion (cheap, and every downstream\n391\t   feature quality depends on it).\n392\t2. Build apps/similarity with K-Means + a nearest-neighbour list; get the\n393\t   similar-players list working and looking right — it's the lower-risk,\n394\t   higher-"wow" half and doesn't depend on nailing the labels.\n395\t3. Is the unmerged bio branch a problem?
Not a blocker — but it shapes how you start. Age is the only requested field that lives only on player-bio-fields (Player.birthDate + its migration); everything else is on main already.
Recommendation: cut the comparisons branch off player-bio-fields, not main. The comparisons PR then stacks on the bio PR and merges after it. Real risks to manage:
    • Migration ordering — bio migration is pending; if we also add the O/D-rebound columns that's a second pending migration. They must stay in a consistent order in the branch chain.
    • If reviewers change the bio API/response shape, rebase.
    • Don't let the stack get deeper — worth nudging the team to finish the bio review.
Alternative: build off main without age, add age in a follow-up once bio lands. Cleaner git, slightly worse UX.
Data audit
Written up in docs/plans/player-comparisons.md. Summary:
Have it (exposed in the API today): points/game, FG%, 3P%, FT%, rebounds (total), assists, steals, blocks, turnovers, games played. Plus team, height, and age (bio branch).
Have the raw data, just not exposed — a few lines in stats.service.ts deriveSeasonAverages: minutes/game, FTA/game, FGA/game, 3PA/game. Note minutes is stored rounded down to whole minutes (games.py:112), so MPG reads slightly low.
Missing — needs ingestion + schema work: the offensive/defensive rebound split. Only reboundsTotal is ingested; BoxScoreTraditionalV3 returns reboundsOffensive/reboundsDefensive in the same response (no extra call), but it needs a PlayerGameStat migration, a games.py change, a re-ingest/backfill, and seed updates. Documented as deferrable — show total rebounds now, slot O/D in later.
Nothing else on your list is missing.

Coding Conventions
Apply these conventions when writing or reviewing code.
Design
    • Follow SOLID object-oriented design principles where applicable: Single Responsibility, Open/Closed, Liskov Substitution, Interface Segregation, Dependency Inversion.
Naming
    • Variables are nouns. Prefer elapsed_time_in_days = 0 over d = 0.
    • Functions are verbs. Prefer update_item_price() over item_updater().
    • Avoid disinformation. Don't name something in a way that misrepresents what it is — e.g. don't call a list an "array", don't name a variable accountList if it's actually a Map.
    • Include units of measurement in variable names when the variable measures something. Prefer time_in_milliseconds over time.
Functions
    • Keep functions small. Each function should do one thing, contain few lines, and prefer a single level of indentation. Treat this as a strong preference, not a hard limit — don't over-extract trivial one-line helpers just to satisfy the rule.
General
    • Avoid magic numbers and strings. Use named constants instead of unexplained literals.
    • Use consistent casing per language convention — e.g. snake_case for Python, camelCase for JavaScript/Java — matching the idiomatic style of the language in use.
Documentation
    • Document code as it's written, not after. Add docstrings/comments in the same pass as writing the function, not as a cleanup step later.
    • Be descriptive about how functions work — explain what the function does, its parameters, return value, and any non-obvious behavior or edge cases, not just a one-line restatement of the function name.
CRUD Methodology
    • Adhere to CRUD (Create, Read, Update, Delete) methodology when designing functions and data-handling code. Each function should map clearly to one of these operations rather than mixing several — e.g. keep create_user(), get_user(), update_user(), and delete_user() as separate functions instead of one function that both creates and updates records.

User
2026/08/29, 20:07:39
Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\shlat.claude\skills\team-git-workflow
Team Git Workflow
Standard git workflow for team projects. Applies any time Claude is helping with git operations — commits, branches, merges, PRs — on a team codebase.
Branches
    • main — the stable, always-up-to-date version of the code. Nothing goes here until it's reviewed and agreed on.
    • Feature branches — one per feature (e.g. a new page, a new endpoint). Branch off main.
        ◦ Examples: applicant-profile-page, cv-upload, nqf-dropdown
        ◦ Keep names short, lowercase, hyphenated.
Committing
Always ask before committing. Never run git commit (or git push) without first showing the proposed commit message(s) and getting an explicit go-ahead. This applies even if the work is clearly finished.
One commit per independent unit of work. A unit of work is a single feature, a single bug fix, a single refactor, etc. — things that don't depend on each other. If a change touches two unrelated things, split it into two commits (and ask about each separately if needed). Don't bundle "add CV upload" and "fix typo in nav" into one commit.
Scoped commits, simple humanized tags. Format:
tag: short plain-English description
    • Tag is one lowercase word, no parentheses/scopes (skip feat(auth): style — keep it simple)
    • Common tags: feat, fix, refactor, chore, docs, style, test
    • Description is imperative, no trailing period: feat: add CV upload button, not feat: Added CV upload button.
    • No walls of text in the subject line — if more detail is needed, put it in the commit body, not the tag line Examples:
    • feat: add NQF qualification dropdown
    • fix: correct SAQA lookup validation
    • refactor: simplify applicant form state
    • chore: bump supabase client version
Checking for sensitive info before committing
Before proposing any commit, check the staged changes for secrets or sensitive data — don't just trust .gitignore. Look for things like:
    • API keys, tokens, or credentials (e.g. Supabase service role keys, JWT secrets)
    • .env files or hardcoded connection strings/passwords
    • Private keys or certificates (.pem, .key, etc.)
    • Any personal data that shouldn't be in the repo Practically:
    1. Run git diff --staged (or check git status for files about to be added) and scan it yourself before writing the commit message.
    2. If something sensitive shows up, stop and flag it — don't commit it, and suggest moving it to a .env file (added to .gitignore) or an untracked config file instead.
    3. If a secret has already been committed in an earlier commit, flag that separately — removing it from the latest commit isn't enough, it's still in git history and the key should be rotated.
Merging back to main
Only merge a feature branch into main once all team members have reviewed the code and agree it's good and finished. Before merging:
    1. Confirm the branch has actually been reviewed (don't assume — ask the user if the team has signed off).
    2. If there's no confirmation that everyone agrees, don't merge — flag it and suggest opening/continuing a PR for review instead.
Quick workflow summary
    1. Branch off main for the feature.
    2. Do the work.
    3. When a self-contained unit of work is done, check the staged changes for secrets/sensitive info, then propose a commit message using the tag format above and ask before committing.
    4. Push the feature branch.
    5. Open for team review (PR or equivalent).
    6. Merge to main only once everyone has reviewed and agreed it's finished.

Throws a 400 when the count falls outside\n// the supported range so the caller gets a clear error rather than a partial\n// or oversized comparison.\nfunction parseComparisonIds(ids: unknown): string[] {\n  const rawIds = typeof ids === "string" ? ids.split(",").map((id) => id.trim()).filter(Boolean) : [];\n  const uniqueIds = [...new Set(rawIds)];\n\n  if (uniqueIds.length < MIN_COMPARISON_PLAYERS || uniqueIds.length > MAX_COMPARISON_PLAYERS) {\n    throw new ApiException(\n      HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST,\n      "BAD_REQUEST",\n      A comparison needs between ${MIN_COMPARISON_PLAYERS} and ${MAX_COMPARISON_PLAYERS} player ids\n    );\n  }\n  return uniqueIds;\n}\n\n@Controller("v1/players")\nexport class PlayersController {\n  constructor(\n    private readonly playersService: PlayersService,\n    private readonly statsService: StatsService\n  ) {}\n\n  // GET /v1/players?teamId=&position=&search=&page=&pageSize= — paginated player list.\n  @Get()\n  listPlayers(@Query() query: Record<string, unknown>) {\n    return this.playersService.getPlayers(query);\n  }\n\n  // GET /v1/players/compare?ids=a,b,c — the same derived season line as\n  // /:id/stats, for 2-4 players at once, so the compare page makes one\n  // request instead of N. Declared before the ":id" route so "compare" is\n  // never swallowed as a player id.\n  @Get("compare")\n  async comparePlayers(@Query("ids") ids: unknown): Promise<{ players: PlayerComparisonEntry[] }> {\n    const playerIds = parseComparisonIds(ids);\n\n    const players = await Promise.all(\n      playerIds.map(async (id) => {\n        const player = await this.playersService.getPlayerById(id);\n        if (!player) {\n          throw new ApiException(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND, "NOT_FOUND", Player ${id} not found);\n        }\n        const seasonAverages = await this.statsService.getPlayerSeasonAverages(id);\n        return { player, seasonAverages };\n      })\n    );\n    return { players };\n  }\n\n  @Get(":id")\n  async getPlayer(@Param("id") id: string) {\n    const player = await this.playersService.getPlayerById(id);\n    if (!player) {\n      throw new ApiException(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND, "NOT_FOUND", "Player not found");\n    }\n    return player;\n  }\n\n  // GET /v1/players/:id/stats — season averages and points-by-game log,\n  // both derived at request time from this player's PlayerGameStat rows.\n  @Get(":id/stats")\n  async getPlayerStats(@Param("id") id: string) {\n    const player = await this.playersService.getPlayerById(id);\n    if (!player) {\n      throw new ApiException(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND, "NOT_FOUND", "Player not found");\n    }\n\n    const [seasonAverages, gameLog] = await Promise.all([\n      this.statsService.getPlayerSeasonAverages(id),\n      this.statsService.getPlayerGameLog(id),\n    ]);\n    return { playerId: id, seasonAverages, gameLog };\n  }\n}\n" }, "caller": { "type": "direct" } } ]

[]).filter(\n    (player) => !excludedPlayerIds.includes(player.id)\n  );\n\n  function selectPlayer(player: Player) {\n    onSelect(player);\n    setSearchTerm("");\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div className="relative w-full">\n      <input\n        aria-label="Search a player to compare"\n        className="w-full rounded-md border border-border-subtle bg-surface-card px-3 py-2 text-sm text-text-primary placeholder:text-text-muted focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-brand-accent/50"\n        type="search"\n        placeholder={placeholder}\n        value={searchTerm}\n        onChange={(event) => setSearchTerm(event.target.value)}\n      />\n\n      {isSearchable && (\n        <ul className="absolute z-10 mt-1 max-h-72 w-full overflow-auto rounded-md border border-border-subtle bg-surface-card shadow-lg">\n          {resultsQuery.isPending ? (\n            <li className="px-3 py-2 text-sm text-text-muted">Searching…\n          ) : results.length === 0 ? (\n            <li className="px-3 py-2 text-sm text-text-muted">No players found.\n          ) : (\n            results.map((player) => (\n              \n                <button\n                  type="button"\n                  className="flex w-full items-center gap-2 px-3 py-2 text-left text-sm text-text-primary hover:bg-surface-raised"\n                  onClick={() => selectPlayer(player)}\n                >\n                  <PlayerHeadshot player={player} size="sm" />\n                  <span className="flex-1">\n                    {player.firstName} {player.lastName}\n                  \n                  {player.team && <TeamBadge team={player.team} size="sm" />}\n                \n              \n            ))\n          )}\n        \n      )}\n    \n  );\n}\n" }, "caller": { "type": "direct" } } ]

className="grid gap-3">\n          <Skeleton className="h-40 w-full" />\n          <Skeleton className="h-40 w-full" />\n        \n      )}\n\n      {activeQuery.isError && (\n        <ErrorState 2026/08/29, 20:22:15
Branch player-comparisons is cut from main and the first pass is implemented.
What changed
API
    • stats.service.ts — DerivedSeasonAverages gains minutesPerGame, and made/attempted-per-game for FG / 3P / FT (all derived from data already in PlayerGameStat).
    • players.controller.ts — new GET /v1/players/compare?ids=a,b,c (2–4 ids, de-duped, order preserved; 400 on bad count, 404 if any id is unknown). Declared before :id so "compare" isn't swallowed as a player id.
    • e2e + unit tests added.
Web
    • ComparePage.tsx at /compare — player tiles (headshot, name, team badge, position, ✕ remove) + an add-by-search tile. Selection is stored in ?ids= so a comparison is shareable and survives reload. Stat rows grouped General / Games / Scoring / Playmaking & defense, with the leader in each row highlighted (turnovers: lower wins).
    • PlayerSearchCombobox.tsx — debounced name search, reused for the add-player tile.
    • PlayerProfilePage.tsx — added MPG tile, FTA/G to shooting splits, and a "Compare" link → /compare?ids=<player>.
    • Compare nav link, route, types, fetchPlayerComparison, tests.
Docs — docs/plans/player-comparisons.md (full data audit) and PROJECT_OVERVIEW route/endpoint tables.
Deferred (documented in the plan)
    • Age — needs Player.birthDate from the unmerged bio branch. Page subheading notes it. Add an Age row once bios merge.
    • Offensive/defensive rebound split — only total rebounds are ingested. BoxScoreTraditionalV3 returns the split in the same response, but it needs a PlayerGameStat migration + re-ingest, so it's its own change. Comparison shows total rebounds.
Verification
    • Web: tsc -b clean, all 108 tests pass, lint clean.
    • API: tsc --noEmit and eslint clean. I could not run the API test suite — its global setup needs the test Postgres and Docker Desktop isn't running here. Please run npm run db:up:test && npm run test --prefix apps/api before pushing.
Nothing is committed yet. Want me to stage and propose commits (I'd split it: API endpoint+derived fields, then the web compare page, then docs)?

[ { "tool_use_id": "toolu_01FRQYRFSdThvAJkxh4Qdiby", "type": "tool_result", "content": "1\t# ADR-003: Hosting topology\n2\t\n3\t- Status: Proposed — awaiting team review before acceptance. Do not\n4\t  implement until the team has signed off (see\n5\t  GIT_METHODOLOGY.md, "Requirements for merging").\n6\t- Date: 2026-08-19\n7\t- Updated: 2026-08-19 — database moved from Azure PostgreSQL Flexible\n8\t  Server to Supabase; frontend moved to Cloudflare Pages; API and batch jobs\n9\t  moved to Render after Azure for Students' region policy blocked Azure Static\n10\t  Web Apps and Fly.io's free tier proved unreliable for long-running always-on\n11\t  compute.\n12\t- Fills: the ADR-003 stub referenced in README.md\n13\t  ("production deployment/hosting generally — not decided") and on the public\n14\t  docs site's Decisions section.\n15\t\n16\t## Context\n17\t\n18\tThe platform runs as two non-monolithic apps that only talk over HTTP, plus a\n19\tPostgres database and a set of Python batch processes — none of which is\n20\tdeployed anywhere yet. Locally everything is run by hand:\n21\t\n22\t| Component | Local | What it is |\n23\t|---|---|---|\n24\t| apps/api | NestJS on localhost:4000 | Long-running Node/Express server |\n25\t| apps/web | Vite dev server on localhost:5173 | React SPA (built output is static dist/) |\n26\t| Postgres | Docker container on 55432 | The only thing the API talks to over the network |\n27\t| apps/ingestion, apps/optimizer, apps/predictor | Run by hand | Python scripts that write straight into Postgres, never through the API |\n28\t| CI | Gitea Actions (sdp.ms.wits.ac.za) | Lint/typecheck/test; no deploy stage |\n29\t\n30\tTwo things about the API are decisive for hosting, and both come straight from\n31\tapps/api/src/main.ts:\n32\t\n33\t1. It is a long-running server, not a stateless function. main.ts builds a\n34\t   plain express() instance, mounts helmet, cors, the BetterAuth handler\n35\t   at /auth/*splat (raw body, ahead of express.json()), and then hands\n36\t   that instance to NestFactory.create(). That bootstrap assumes a single\n37\t   persistent process that owns its routes for the lifetime of the server.\n38\t2. It relies on session cookies across origins. CORS is configured with\n39\t   credentials: true and origin: process.env.WEB_ORIGIN, and BetterAuth\n40\t   issues session cookies and runs the Google OAuth redirect dance. Both assume\n41\t   a process that stays warm and a stable, addressable origin.\n42\t\n43\tThe database is Postgres 16 (currently postgres:16-alpine in\n44\tdocker-compose.yml). The brief requires the API remain the only thing that\n45\ttalks to the database over HTTP-facing requests; the Python apps write straight\n46\tinto Postgres as separate processes. The repo's remote is Gitea, not GitHub, so\n47\tany CI/CD deploy step runs on the self-hosted act_runner — GitHub-Actions-only\n48\tdeploy integrations are not directly usable.\n49\t\n50\tAn initial investigation picked Microsoft Azure for compute (Static Web Apps\n51\tfor the SPA, App Service for the API, Container Apps for batch jobs). However,\n52\tthe Azure for Students subscription is governed by a "best available regions"\n53\tpolicy that blocks Azure Static Web Apps in every region offered by the SWA\n54\tcreation wizard (RequestDisallowedByAzure). Microsoft does not grant\n55\tregion-policy exceptions for student subscriptions, and App Service / Container\n56\tApps are at risk of the same restriction. A subsequent look at Fly.io showed\n57\tthat its free tier is not a reliable long-term home for an always-on Machine\n58\tthat must stay warm for the full duration of the course. The team therefore\n59\tpivoted to Render for compute.\n60\t\n61\tWe need to pick where each of these components runs in production and how they\n62\tare wired together, so that a deploy stage can be added to CI and the platform\n63\tcan actually be hosted.\n64\t\n65\t## Decision\n66\t\n67\tHost the static frontend on Cloudflare Pages, the NestJS API and Python batch\n68\tjobs on Render, and the Postgres database on Supabase (managed Postgres —\n69\tSupabase's auto REST/Auth/Storage APIs deliberately unused). Keeping\n70\tSupabase's HTTP API off means the NestJS API stays the only HTTP path to the\n71\tdata, per the brief.\n72\t\n73\t### Topology\n74\t\n75\t\n76\t                        Internet (HTTPS)\n77\t                             |\n78\t        +--------------------+--------------------+\n79\t        |                                         |\n80\t  +-----------+                          +-----------------+\n81\t  | Web SPA   |  Cloudflare Pages        | API             |  Render\n82\t  | (Vite ->  |  (global static CDN,     | (NestJS/Express |  (free\n83\t  |  dist/)   |   managed TLS,           |  +BetterAuth)   |   web service)\n84\t  +-----------+   custom domain)         +-----------------+\n85\t        |                                         |\n86\t        |  /api/*  (CORS w/ credentials,          |\n87\t        |   session cookies, SameSite=None+Secure)|\n88\t        +-----------------+---------------------+\n89\t                          |\n90\t                +---------+---------+\n91\t                |                   |\n92\t        +---------------+   +-----------------------------+\n93\t        | Supabase      |   | Batch jobs                  |\n94\t        | Postgres      |   | Render Cron / Background    |\n95\t        | (managed;     |   | Worker — ingestion,         |\n96\t        | REST APIs     |   | optimizer, predictor        |\n97\t        | unused;       |   | (write straight to Postgres)|\n98\t        | pooled+TLS)   |   +-----------------------------+\n99\t        +---------------+\n100\t\n101\t\n102\t### Component mapping\n103\t\n104\t| Layer | Service | Why this service |\n105\t|---|---|---|\n106\t| Frontend SPA | Cloudflare Pages | apps/web builds to static dist/. Cloudflare Pages gives a global CDN, managed TLS, custom domain support, and a generous free tier. The repo is on Gitea, so deploy is a local build followed by Wrangler CLI upload or dashboard drag-and-drop — no Git-provider integration required. |\n107\t| NestJS API | Render Web Service (Node.js runtime, free plan) | Render's free plan spins the API down after 15 minutes of inactivity; the team has accepted the resulting ~30-second cold start. BetterAuth sessions are stored in Supabase, so they survive a cold start. Render's Blueprint (render.yaml) declares the service and build/start commands. Prisma migrations must be run manually (or from CI) because preDeployCommand is not available on Render's free tier. |\n108\t| Postgres | Supabase (managed Postgres) | Managed Postgres with a generous free tier and built-in connection pooling. Supabase's auto REST/Auth/Storage APIs are deliberately unused — the NestJS API stays the only HTTP path to the data, per the brief. Reached over TLS via the pooled connection string. |\n109\t| Python batch apps | Render Cron Job or Background Worker | apps/ingestion/optimizer/predictor are scripts that write straight to Postgres. They run on a schedule (Render Cron) or as an always-on/off Worker — no long-running web server needed, and they stay off the API's HTTP path as the brief requires. |\n110\t| Secrets | Render env vars + Cloudflare Pages env vars | DATABASE_URL, GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID/GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET, BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, BETTER_AUTH_URL, and WEB_ORIGIN are set in the Render Dashboard for the API and via the Cloudflare Pages dashboard for the build-time VITE_API_BASE_URL. They are never in the repo or the image. |\n111\t| Deploy source | Gitea repo mirrored to GitHub, or Render deploy hook | Render does not natively integrate with Gitea. The simplest path is a Gitea → GitHub mirror (Gitea can keep it in sync) so Render auto-deploys on push; alternatively, trigger deploys via Render's deploy hook from a Gitea Actions workflow. |\n112\t\n113\t### Environment / networking that this implies\n114\t\n115\t- VITE_API_BASE_URL (build-time, for apps/web) → the Render API URL\n116\t  (https://<api-app>.onrender.com or custom domain).\n117\t- WEB_ORIGIN (API CORS) → the Cloudflare Pages production URL\n118\t  (https://<site>.pages.dev or custom domain).\n119\t- BETTER_AUTH_URL → the Render API origin (same as VITE_API_BASE_URL\n120\t  without a path prefix).\n121\t- BetterAuth Google OAuth authorised redirect URI →\n122\t  https://<api-domain>/auth/callback/google (add alongside the existing\n123\t  http://localhost:4000/auth/callback/google).\n124\t- Session cookies → Secure + SameSite=None so they survive the\n125\t  cross-origin (web-domain ↔ api-domain) credentials: true flow.\n126\t- DB access → Supabase pooled connection string (PgBouncer, transaction mode)\n127\t  over TLS; the API and batch workers reach Supabase over the public internet\n128\t  (Supabase IP allow-list optional). No private network because the DB is a\n129\t  separate provider.\n130\t- Local dev is unchanged — Docker Compose Postgres on 55432/55433, npm\n131\t  run dev for both apps. Production is a parallel set of managed services, not\n132\t  a replacement for the local setup.\n133\t\n134\t## Alternatives considered\n135\t\n136\t### Azure\n137\t\n138\tOriginally chosen for the SPA (Static Web Apps), API (App Service), batch jobs\n139\t(Container Apps), secrets (Key Vault), and images (Container Registry). The\n140\tAzure for Students subscription has a "best available regions" policy that\n141\tblocks Static Web Apps in every region offered by the SWA creation wizard\n142\t(RequestDisallowedByAzure). Microsoft does not grant region-policy\n143\texceptions for student subscriptions, and App Service / Container Apps are at\n144\trisk of the same restriction. Azure was therefore abandoned for compute.\n145\t\n146\t### Fly.io\n147\t\n148\tConsidered for the API and batch jobs because its Machines can be configured\n149\tto stay always-on. However, Fly.io's free tier is an allowance, not a\n150\tguaranteed permanent free always-on compute tier; for a project that must stay\n151\tonline longer than the free allowance covers, it would incur billing. The team\n152\tpreferred Render's free tier and has accepted the ~30-second cold start after\n153\t15 minutes of inactivity.\n154\t\n155\t### Render free tier\n156\t\n157\tRender's free Web Service tier spins down after 15 minutes of inactivity and\n158\ttakes ~30 seconds to cold-start on the next request. The team has accepted\n159\tthis cold-start latency to keep the API hosting free. The Starter plan\n160\t($7/month) would eliminate cold starts but was not chosen.\n161\t\n162\t### Vercel\n163\t\n164\tConsidered first for the SPA because of its simple DX. Vercel's compute model\n165\tis serverless functions, which is a poor fit for the long-running NestJS API.\n166\tVercel could host the static frontend, but Cloudflare Pages offers the same\n167\tbenefits with a simpler Gitea-compatible deploy path, so Vercel was not chosen.\n168\t\n169\t## Consequences\n170\t\n171\tPositive\n172\t\n173\t- The API is free to host on Render's free tier; the team has accepted the\n174\t  ~30-second cold start after 15 minutes of inactivity. BetterAuth sessions\n175\t  are stored in Supabase, so they survive a cold start.\n176\t- The API runs as the long-running process main.ts was written to be — no\n177\t  serverless rewrites.\n178\t- Postgres is managed and backed up on Supabase, with built-in connection\n179\t  pooling and a free tier.\n180\t- Frontend is served from Cloudflare's global edge network.\n181\t- Local development stays exactly as it is; production is a parallel managed\n182\t  set, not a replacement.\n183\t- The Azure region-policy problem is completely avoided.\n184\t\n185\tNegative\n186\t\n187\t- Cost: the API is on Render's free tier. Batch jobs may add cost if they run\n188\t  as Render Cron Jobs / Background Workers. The frontend (Cloudflare Pages)\n189\t  and database (Supabase free tier) remain free.\n190\t- Multi-provider stack (Cloudflare + Render + Supabase) means three dashboards\n191\t  and three secret stores instead of one.\n192\t- No Git-provider auto-deploy because the repo is on Gitea — deploys need a\n193\t  Gitea → GitHub mirror or a Gitea Actions workflow that calls Render's deploy\n194\t  hook.\n195\t- Cross-origin cookies need SameSite=None; Secure, which must be set\n196\t  correctly or sign-in silently breaks in production.\n197\t\n198\tNeutral / follow-ups (out of scope for this ADR)\n199\t\n200\t- A deploy stage in .gitea/workflows/ci.yml (build web → upload to\n201\t  Cloudflare Pages; trigger Render deploy for the API; deploy batch jobs) —\n202\t  separate work once this ADR is accepted.\n203\t- render.yaml entries for the Python batch Cron Jobs / Background Workers.\n204\t- Production env/secrets mapping (DATABASE_URL, WEB_ORIGIN,\n205\t  GOOGLE_CLIENT_*, BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, BETTER_AUTH_URL, PORT,\n206\t  VITE_API_BASE_URL).\n207\t- Prisma migrations: on the free tier Render does not run preDeployCommand,\n208\t  so npx prisma migrate deploy must be run manually or from a Gitea Actions\n209\t  workflow before the API deploy.\n210\t- Updating PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md "Known gaps" and\n211\t  README.md to mark hosting as decided once accepted.\n212\t\n213\t## Open questions for the team\n214\t\n215\t1. Render region: default is Oregon; does latency to South Africa warrant\n216\t   mirroring the Gitea repo to a GitHub org closer to the team, or choosing a\n217\t   Render region nearer to Europe?\n218\t2. Batch scheduling: one Render Background Worker that runs the three Python\n219\t   scripts on a cron schedule, or three separate Render Cron Jobs?\n220\t3. Domains: custom domains for both apps, or *.onrender.com +\n221\t   *.pages.dev for the demo?\n222\t4. Supabase free tier caps the DB at ~500 MB and pauses after inactivity —\n223\t   enough for the demo/mock data, but does real full-league ingestion need a\n224\t   paid Supabase plan?\n225\t\n226\t## References\n227\t\n228\t- apps/api/src/main.ts — the Express/BetterAuth\n229\t  bootstrap that drives the long-running-server requirement.\n230\t- render.yaml — the Render Blueprint for the API.\n231\t- docker-compose.yml — local Postgres setup this\n232\t  parallels.\n233\t- docs/PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md — tech stack and the\n234\t  "Production deployment — not done" known gap.\n235\t- docs/GIT_METHODOLOGY.md — review gate this ADR's\n236\t  Proposed status respects.\n237\t" } ]

It needs the\n40\t  // raw, unparsed body, so it's mounted ahead of express.json().\n41\t  server.all("/auth/*splat", toNodeHandler(auth));\n42\t\n43\t  server.use(expressFactory.json());\n44\t\n45\t  const app = await NestFactory.create(AppModule, new ExpressAdapter(server), { bodyParser: false });\n46\t  app.useGlobalFilters(new AllExceptionsFilter());\n47\t\n48\t  const port = Number(process.env.PORT) || DEFAULT_PORT;\n49\t  await app.listen(port);\n50\t  console.log(API listening on http://localhost:${port});\n51\t}\n52\t\n53\tbootstrap();\n54\t" } ]

If it doesn't, that's a bug in this page — fix it, don't just work around it.\n4\t\n5\t## Prerequisites\n6\t\n7\t- Node.js (LTS, v24+) and npm — for both apps/api (NestJS) and apps/web (React + Vite)\n8\t- Docker and Docker Compose — runs Postgres locally\n9\t- Git, with access to the project's Gitea repository\n10\t- Python 3 — only if you're working on the nba_api ingestion piece (apps/ingestion), the predictor (apps/predictor), or the optimizer (apps/optimizer)\n11\t\n12\t## 1. Clone the repo\n13\t\n14\tbash\n15\tgit clone https://sdp.ms.wits.ac.za/innovation/sportsanalytics.git\n16\tcd sportsanalytics\n17\t\n18\t\n19\t## 2. Set up environment variables\n20\t\n21\tbash\n22\tcp apps/api/.env.example apps/api/.env\n23\t\n24\t\n25\tFill in the values .env.example documents. The required variables are:\n26\t\n27\t| Variable | Purpose |\n28\t|---|---|\n29\t| DATABASE_URL | Postgres connection string (default: postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:55432/nba_analytics?schema=public) |\n30\t| PORT | API port (default: 4000) |\n31\t| WEB_ORIGIN | CORS origin for the frontend (default: http://localhost:5173, comma-separated for multiple) |\n32\t| BETTER_AUTH_SECRET | Generate with openssl rand -base64 32 |\n33\t| BETTER_AUTH_URL | API origin (default: http://localhost:4000) |\n34\t| GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID | Google OAuth client ID — create at Google Cloud Console |\n35\t| GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET | Google OAuth client secret |\n36\t| VITE_API_BASE_URL | (frontend, if needed) API base URL for production builds |\n37\t\n38\tNever commit the filled-in .env files. See Security for why.\n39\t\n40\t## 3. Start Postgres\n41\t\n42\tbash\n43\tdocker compose up -d\n44\t\n45\t\n46\tThis brings up Postgres locally so both apps can connect to it. Confirm it's running with docker compose ps.\n47\t\n48\t## 4. Set up the database\n49\t\n50\tFrom apps/api:\n51\t\n52\tbash\n53\tcd apps/api\n54\tnpm install\n55\tnpx prisma migrate dev\n56\tnpx prisma db seed   # loads mock NBA seed data\n57\t\n58\t\n59\t## 5. Run the backend\n60\t\n61\tbash\n62\tnpm run start:dev\n63\t\n64\t\n65\tThe NestJS API should now be running at http://localhost:4000. The health check at /health should return { \"status\": \"ok\" }.\n66\t\n67\t## 6. Run the frontend\n68\t\n69\tFrom apps/web, in a separate terminal:\n70\t\n71\tbash\n72\tcd apps/web\n73\tnpm install\n74\tnpm run dev\n75\t\n76\t\n77\tVite's dev server will print the local URL (default http://localhost:5173). The Vite config proxies /api/* to http://localhost:4000, so the frontend reaches the backend transparently.\n78\t\n79\t## 7. Run tests\n80\t\n81\tbash\n82\t# from apps/api (Vitest + Supertest, integration tests run against a real disposable Postgres DB)\n83\tnpm run test\n84\t\n85\t# from apps/web (Vitest + React Testing Library)\n86\tnpm run test\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t## 8. Run the same checks CI runs\n90\t\n91\tBefore pushing, run locally what CI will run on your branch — a failure here is a failure there:\n92\t\n93\tbash\n94\t# apps/api\n95\tnpm ci\n96\tnpx prisma generate     # required before typecheck; needs no database\n97\tnpm run lint            # ESLint 10, flat config\n98\tnpx tsc --noEmit\n99\t\n100\t# apps/web\n101\tnpm ci\n102\tnpm run lint            # oxlint\n103\tnpx tsc -b --noEmit     # -b is required: tsconfig.json is solution-style\n104\t\n105\t\n106\t## Troubleshooting\n107\t\n108\t| Problem | Likely cause |\n109\t|---|---|\n110\t| API can't connect to Postgres | Docker Compose isn't running, or the connection string in apps/api/.env doesn't match the Compose service | \n111\t| Prisma migration fails | Database not reachable yet — wait a few seconds after docker compose up -d before migrating, or check docker compose logs |\n112\t| Frontend shows no data / requests to /api/... 404 or fail with a CORS error | Confirmed working via vite.config.ts's /api → http://localhost:4000 proxy — if you're still seeing this, check the backend is actually running on port 4000. |\n113\t| Google sign-in fails locally | Check GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID / GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET are set in apps/api/.env and the redirect URI (http://localhost:4000/auth/callback/google) is registered in the Google Cloud console. |\n114\t| tsc reports "Cannot find module" errors in apps/api for packages that are in package.json | Stale local install. Run npm ci && npm run prisma:generate in apps/api. |\n115\t\n116\tIf you hit something not covered here, add it to this table once you've solved it — that's the point of this page.\n117\t\n118\t---\n119\t\n120\tAI Declaration: The preceding document was generated with the assistance of the following: Claude-Web[Claude Sonnet 5]\n121\t" } ]

[ { "tool_use_id": "toolu_01Pf29heJMz1YrZsz2GAufMU", "type": "tool_result", "content": "1\t# Git Methodology\n2\t\n3\tHow this team uses git: when to commit, how to write commits, when to\n4\tbranch, how to name branches, when to merge, what's required before a\n5\tmerge, and when/how releases are versioned.\n6\t\n7\t## When to commit\n8\t\n9\tCommit once a single, independent unit of work is done — one feature, one\n10\tbug fix, one refactor. Don't bundle unrelated changes into the same commit\n11\t(e.g. "add Teams page" and "fix a typo in the README" are two commits, not\n12\tone). If a change touches two unrelated things, split it.\n13\t\n14\tBefore committing:\n15\t\n16\t- Check the diff (git diff --staged) for secrets or sensitive data —\n17\t  API keys, tokens, .env contents, credentials, private keys, personal\n18\t  data. Don't trust .gitignore alone; actually look.\n19\t- If AI assistance (e.g. Claude Code) prepared the change, it shows the\n20\t  proposed commit message and waits for an explicit go-ahead before\n21\t  running git commit — never committed silently on its own.\n22\t\n23\t## How to write commits\n24\t\n25\t\n26\ttag: short plain-English description\n27\t\n28\t\n29\t- Tag: one lowercase word, no scopes/parens (skip feat(auth): style).\n30\t  Common tags: feat, fix, refactor, chore, docs, style, test.\n31\t- Description: imperative mood, no trailing period —\n32\t  feat: add CV upload button, not feat: Added CV upload button.\n33\t- Keep the subject line short. If more detail is needed, put it in the\n34\t  commit body, not the tag line.\n35\t\n36\tExamples:\n37\t\n38\t- refactor: migrate API backend from Express to NestJS\n39\t- feat: replace Passport auth with BetterAuth (Google OAuth)\n40\t- fix: correct auth status codes and remove magic numbers in API\n41\t- chore: bump prisma client version\n42\t\n43\t## When to branch\n44\t\n45\tBranch for every feature, fix, or other independent unit of work — never\n46\tcommit directly to main. Branch off main.\n47\t\n48\t## How to name branches\n49\t\n50\tShort, lowercase, hyphenated. Name it after what it does, not who's doing\n51\tit or a ticket number.\n52\t\n53\tExamples: nestjs-migration, betterauth-google-oauth, frontend-ui-teams.\n54\t\n55\t## When to merge\n56\t\n57\tOnly once every team member has reviewed the branch and agreed it's\n58\tgood and finished — never merge on the strength of one person's opinion,\n59\tincluding the author's. If there's no confirmation that everyone agrees,\n60\tdon't merge; open (or keep open) a PR for review instead.\n61\t\n62\t## Requirements for merging\n63\t\n64\tBefore a branch merges into main:\n65\t\n66\t1. The team has actually reviewed it — confirmed, not assumed.\n67\t2. No secrets or sensitive data anywhere in the diff.\n68\t3. It typechecks, lints, and builds cleanly.\n69\t4. A PR exists on Gitea so the review has a record (<branch> -> main).\n70\t\n71\tmain stays the stable, always-working version of the code — nothing\n72\tlands there until the above is true.\n73\t\n74\t## When to version\n75\t\n76\tAt the end of each sprint, once that sprint's approved work is merged into\n77\tmain, tag the resulting commit as a release. Versioning isn't done\n78\tad hoc between sprints — one tag per sprint keeps the release history easy\n79\tto follow and lines up with the SDP's own milestone cadence.\n80\t\n81\t## How to version\n82\t\n83\tReleases are tagged by date (CalVer), not semantic major/minor/patch\n84\tnumbers — simpler to reason about than deciding what counts as "breaking"\n85\ton a project whose scope is still evolving sprint to sprint.\n86\t\n87\tFormat: vYYYY.MM.DD, the date the sprint's release is cut — e.g.\n88\tv2026.08.13. If more than one release lands on the same date (rare),\n89\tappend .1, .2, etc.: v2026.08.13.1.\n90\t\n91\tbash\n92\tgit checkout main\n93\tgit pull origin main\n94\tgit tag -a v2026.08.13 -m \"Sprint 3: NestJS + BetterAuth migration, Teams UI\"\n95\tgit push origin v2026.08.13\n96\t\n97\t\n98\tThe tag message is a one-line, plain-English summary of what shipped that\n99\tsprint — same tone as a commit description, not a changelog dump.\n100\t" } ]

Assistant
2026/08/29, 20:46:35
Before any of that — I checked, and this branch changes no schema at all:
git diff --stat main...HEAD -- apps/api/prisma/
is empty. The comparison feature only adds request-time derived fields and a new endpoint — no schema.prisma edit, no migration, no _prisma_migrations change. So for player-comparisons specifically there is nothing to migrate and zero drift risk. The plan below is the reusable process for any branch that does touch schema.prisma (the next one that adds the O/D rebound columns will need it).
What actually went wrong with PR #47
The 20260820103112_player_bio_fields migration file was committed. The break was that nothing ran npx prisma migrate deploy against Supabase on deploy:
    • Render free tier has no preDeployCommand (render.yaml:14)
    • CI has no deploy stage (ADR-003 §207)
    • main.ts on main does not run migrations on boot (that fix, dbf7bbd, only exists on the unmerged bio branch)
So new API code (Prisma client with birthDate) hit a prod DB without those columns → runtime errors → panic revert.
That means there are two independent things to verify before merging a schema change:
    • A — completeness: every schema.prisma change has a matching migration file (no drift)
    • B — safety on real data: migrate deploy applies cleanly on top of the actual prod schema + data
Local test plan (for a branch that changes the schema)
0. Setup
Docker Postgres up (docker compose up -d), apps/api/.env → local dev DB.
1. Drift check — "did I forget to generate a migration?"
cd apps/api
# empty scratch DB for prisma to replay migrations into
docker exec -i nba-analytics-postgres psql -U postgres -c "DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS prisma_shadow; CREATE DATABASE prisma_shadow;"

npx prisma migrate diff \
  --from-migrations ./prisma/migrations \
  --to-schema-datamodel ./prisma/schema.prisma \
  --shadow-database-url "postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:55432/prisma_shadow" \
  --exit-code
Exit 0 = migration files fully describe schema.prisma. Exit 2 = stop, you changed the schema without prisma migrate dev — this is exactly the class of bug you're worried about.
2. Get a copy of prod (never run migrations against prod as a "test")
Use Supabase's direct connection string (port 5432, not the 6543 pooler), public schema only:
docker exec nba-analytics-postgres pg_dump "$SUPABASE_DIRECT_URL" \
  --schema=public --no-owner --no-privileges > prod_public.sql

docker exec -i nba-analytics-postgres psql -U postgres -c "DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS prod_replica; CREATE DATABASE prod_replica;"
docker exec -i nba-analytics-postgres psql -U postgres -d prod_replica < prod_public.sql
The dump includes _prisma_migrations, so the replica starts from prod's exact migration baseline.
3. Run the real deploy step against the replica
DATABASE_URL="postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:55432/prod_replica?schema=public" \
  npx prisma migrate deploy
This is byte-for-byte what must run against Supabase. Check:
    • the new migration is listed as applied
    • no P3009 (failed migration in history), no lock timeout
    • a NOT NULL column added to a populated table with no default/backfill fails here, on real row counts — the catch you want
Then confirm the migrated replica matches the schema:
npx prisma migrate status   # "Database schema is up to date!"
DATABASE_URL=...prod_replica... npx prisma migrate diff \
  --from-schema-datamodel ./prisma/schema.prisma --to-url "$DATABASE_URL" --exit-code
4. Boot the new API against the migrated replica
DATABASE_URL=...prod_replica... npm run start:dev
curl localhost:4000/health
# exercise the endpoints this branch changed, against real data shapes
5 & 6. Full test suites (npm test in apps/api + apps/web) and the CI checks (getting-started §8).
Make it impossible to forget (I can do these)
    1. GIT_METHODOLOGY "Requirements for merging" — add: if schema.prisma changed, the PR must contain a migration file and record that steps 1–3 above passed against a prod replica.
    2. apps/api/package.json — add "migrate:deploy": "prisma migrate deploy" and a "migrate:check" script wrapping the step-1 drift check.
    3. CI drift gate — a job that runs prisma migrate deploy against the CI Postgres then the migrate diff ... --exit-code. Fails the build on drift.
