Shared by the tile header and every stat group so all\n// three line up on one grid.\nconst LABEL_COLUMN_WIDTH = "11rem";\n\nfunction formatHeight(heightInches: number | null): string {\n  if (heightInches === null) return "—";\n  return ${Math.floor(heightInches / 12)}'${heightInches % 12}\";\n}\n\n// "6.4 / 13.5 (47.5%)" — made, attempted and accuracy in one cell, so the\n// shooting rows don't need three separate labels each.\nfunction formatShootingSplit(made: number, attempted: number, percentage: number): string {\n  return ${made} / ${attempted} (${percentage}%);\n}\n\n// One comparable metric: how to display it per player, and (optionally) the\n// numeric value used to highlight the leader in that row.\ninterface StatRow {\n  label: string;\n  render: (averages: SeasonAverages, player: Player) => string;\n  compareValue?: (averages: SeasonAverages) => number;\n  // Turnovers are the one row where a lower number is the better one.\n  higherIsBetter?: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface StatGroup {\n  title: string;\n  // Optional line under the heading explaining a shared cell format.\n  caption?: string;\n  rows: StatRow[];\n}\n\n// Grouped so each heading carries the "(per game)" qualifier and the row\n// names underneath stay short.\nconst STAT_GROUPS: StatGroup[] = [\n  {\n    title: "General",\n    rows: [\n      { label: "Team", render: (_averages, player) => player.team?.name ?? "—" },\n      { label: "Position", render: (_averages, player) => player.position },\n      { label: "Height", render: (_averages, player) => formatHeight(player.heightInches) },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Games",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Total played",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.gamesPlayed},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.gamesPlayed,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Minutes per game",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.minutesPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.minutesPerGame,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Points (per game)",\n    caption: "made / attempted (accuracy)",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Total",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.pointsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.pointsPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Field goals",\n        render: (averages) =>\n          formatShootingSplit(\n            averages.fieldGoalsMadePerGame,\n            averages.fieldGoalsAttemptedPerGame,\n            averages.fieldGoalPercentage\n          ),\n        // Leader is whoever makes more per game, not whoever shoots the\n        // better percentage — these rows sit under "Points", so they're\n        // about scoring production. Accuracy is shown alongside so a\n        // low-volume, high-efficiency line is still readable.\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.fieldGoalsMadePerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "3 pointers",\n        render: (averages) =>\n          formatShootingSplit(\n            averages.threesMadePerGame,\n            averages.threesAttemptedPerGame,\n            averages.threePointPercentage\n          ),\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.threesMadePerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Free throws",\n        render: (averages) =>\n          formatShootingSplit(\n            averages.freeThrowsMadePerGame,\n            averages.freeThrowsAttemptedPerGame,\n            averages.freeThrowPercentage\n          ),\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.freeThrowsMadePerGame,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Rebounds & assists (per game)",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Rebounds",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.reboundsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.reboundsPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Assists",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.assistsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.assistsPerGame,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Defense & ball security (per game)",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Steals",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.stealsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.stealsPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Blocks",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.blocksPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.blocksPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Turnovers",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.turnoversPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.turnoversPerGame,\n        higherIsBetter: false,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\n// Indexes of the entries holding the best value for this row. Empty when\n// the row isn't comparable, there are fewer than two players, or every\n// player has the same value (nothing to single out).\nfunction bestEntryIndexes(entries: PlayerComparisonEntry[], row: StatRow): Set {\n  if (!row.compareValue || entries.length < MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_COMPARISON) return new Set();\n\n  const values = entries.map((entry) => row.compareValue!(entry.seasonAverages));\n  const allEqual = values.every((value) => value === values[0]);\n  if (allEqual) return new Set();\n\n  const higherIsBetter = row.higherIsBetter ?? true;\n  const best = higherIsBetter ? Math.max(...values) : Math.min(...values);\n  return new Set(values.flatMap((value, index) => (value === best ? [index] : [])));\n}\n\nfunction useSelectedPlayerIds(): [string[], (playerIds: string[]) => void] {\n  const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();\n  const playerIds = useMemo(() => {\n    const raw = searchParams.get("ids");\n    return raw ? 2026/08/29, 22:00:04
Reworked ComparePage.tsx to match the reference layout.
One grid, shared by everything. The header row and every stat group use the same gridTemplateColumns: 11rem repeat(n, 1fr), so tiles, row names and values line up on identical column edges — no drift between sections.
Left column. "Add another player" is now a compact button (circled + above the label, accent-coloured) sitting at the top of the label column, above the row names, exactly as in your example. Clicking it swaps the button for the search field in place. Row names below are left-aligned; everything else is centred.
Headings carry the qualifier. Centred, uppercase, letter-spaced — consistent with the nav's type treatment:
    • General — team, position, height
    • Games — total played, minutes per game
    • Points (per game) — total, field goals, 3 pointers, free throws
    • Rebounds & assists (per game) — rebounds, assists
    • Defense & ball security (per game) — steals, blocks, turnovers
Shooting rows are one cell each, made / attempted (accuracy) — e.g. 6.4 / 13.5 (47.5%) — with a small caption under the Points heading. That keeps the free-throw attempts you originally asked for visible without three rows per shot type.
One judgement call worth flagging: for those combined rows the highlight goes to higher makes per game, not the better percentage. That's what your reference does (Booker is highlighted at 8.5 (45.6%) over 6.4 (47.5%) — more makes, worse accuracy), and it's consistent with the rows sitting under "Points". Noted in a code comment and in the plan doc.
Type/visuals. Values are tabular-nums so columns of digits align; leader values get an accent pill (bg-brand-accent/15) rather than just coloured text; each value sits in its own rounded surface-card cell; tiles are bordered cards with the ✕ in the top-left.
Also fixed a real bug I introduced earlier: the empty state was gated on !activeQuery.isPending, but a disabled TanStack Query still reports isPending: true, so with zero players selected it would never have rendered. Now keyed off the selection.
Verified: tsc -b clean, oxlint clean, full web suite 109 passed (added a test for the shooting-split format and the leader rule; updated the add-player tests for the click-to-reveal behaviour).
API side is unchanged, so it still needs npm run db:up:test && npm run test --prefix apps/api on your machine — Docker isn't available here. Nothing committed yet.

Shared by the tile header and every stat group so all of them sit\n// on one column grid; clamped rather than fixed so narrow screens give the\n// player columns their space back without a media query.\nconst LABEL_COLUMN_WIDTH = "clamp(5.5rem, 16vw, 11rem)";\n\nfunction formatHeight(heightInches: number | null): string {\n  if (heightInches === null) return "—";\n  return ${Math.floor(heightInches / 12)}'${heightInches % 12}\";\n}\n\n// "6.4 / 13.5 (47.5%)" — made, attempted and accuracy in one cell, so the\n// shooting rows need one label each instead of three.\nfunction formatShootingSplit(made: number, attempted: number, percentage: number): string {\n  return ${made} / ${attempted} (${percentage}%);\n}\n\n// One comparable metric: how to display it per player, and (optionally) the\n// numeric value used to highlight the leader in that row.\ninterface StatRow {\n  label: string;\n  render: (averages: SeasonAverages, player: Player) => string;\n  compareValue?: (averages: SeasonAverages) => number;\n  // Turnovers are the one row where a lower number is the better one.\n  higherIsBetter?: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface StatGroup {\n  title: string;\n  // Optional line under the heading explaining a shared cell format.\n  caption?: string;\n  rows: StatRow[];\n}\n\n// Grouped so each heading carries the "(per game)" qualifier and the row\n// names underneath stay short.\nconst STAT_GROUPS: StatGroup[] = [\n  {\n    title: "General",\n    rows: [\n      { label: "Team", render: (_averages, player) => player.team?.name ?? Accuracy shows alongside so a\n        // low-volume, high-efficiency line still reads correctly.\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.fieldGoalsMadePerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "3 pointers",\n        render: (averages) =>\n          formatShootingSplit(\n            averages.threesMadePerGame,\n            averages.threesAttemptedPerGame,\n            averages.threePointPercentage\n          ),\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.threesMadePerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Free throws",\n        render: (averages) =>\n          formatShootingSplit(\n            averages.freeThrowsMadePerGame,\n            averages.freeThrowsAttemptedPerGame,\n            averages.freeThrowPercentage\n          ),\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.freeThrowsMadePerGame,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Rebounds & assists (per game)",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Rebounds",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.reboundsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.reboundsPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Assists",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.assistsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.assistsPerGame,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    title: "Defense & ball security (per game)",\n    rows: [\n      {\n        label: "Steals",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.stealsPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.stealsPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Blocks",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.blocksPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.blocksPerGame,\n      },\n      {\n        label: "Turnovers",\n        render: (averages) => ${averages.turnoversPerGame},\n        compareValue: (averages) => averages.turnoversPerGame,\n        higherIsBetter: false,\n      },\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\n// Indexes of the entries holding the best value for this row. Empty when the\n// row isn't comparable, there are fewer than two players, or every player has\n// the same value (nothing to single out).\nfunction bestEntryIndexes(entries: PlayerComparisonEntry[], row: StatRow): Set {\n  const compareValue = row.compareValue;\n  if (!compareValue || entries.length < MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_COMPARISON) return new Set();\n\n  const values = entries.map((entry) => compareValue(entry.seasonAverages));\n  const allEqual = values.every((value) => value === values[0]);\n  if (allEqual) return new Set();\n\n  const higherIsBetter = row.higherIsBetter ?? true;\n  const best = higherIsBetter ? Math.max(...values) : Math.min(...values);\n  return new Set(values.flatMap((value, index) => (value === best ? [index] : [])));\n}\n\nfunction useSelectedPlayerIds(): [string[], (playerIds: string[]) => void] {\n  const [searchParams, setSearchParams] = useSearchParams();\n  const playerIds = useMemo(() => {\n    const raw = searchParams.get("ids");\n    return raw ? [...new Set(raw.split(",").filter(Boolean))] : [];\n  }, [searchParams]);\n\n  function setPlayerIds(nextPlayerIds: string[]) {\n    const nextParams = new URLSearchParams(searchParams);\n    if (nextPlayerIds.length === 0) {\n      nextParams.delete("ids");\n    } else {\n      nextParams.set("ids", nextPlayerIds.join(","));\n    }\n    setSearchParams(nextParams, { replace: true });\n  }\n\n  return [playerIds, setPlayerIds];\n}\n\n// The label column plus one equal column per slot, so the header tiles, the\n// group headings and every stat row share the same column edges.\nfunction gridStyle(slotCount: number) {\n  return {\n    gridTemplateColumns: ${LABEL_COLUMN_WIDTH} repeat(${slotCount}, minmax(0, 1fr)),\n  };\n}\n\n// Group headings sit centred over the player columns only — the label column\n// is excluded, so a heading lands on the gap between two tiles rather than\n// off to the left of centre.\nconst PLAYER_COLUMNS_SPAN = { gridColumn: "2 / -1" };\n\nfunction PlaceholderHeadshot() {\n  return (\n    <div className="flex size-20 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-surface-raised text-text-muted">\n      <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" aria-hidden="true" className="size-10" fill="currentColor">\n        <circle cx="12" cy="8" r="4" />\n        <path d="M4 21c0-4.418 3.582-8 8-8s8 3.582 8 8z" />\n      \n    \n  );\n}\n\nfunction PlayerTile({ player, onRemove }: { player: Player; onRemove: () => void }) {\n  return (\n    <div className="relative flex flex-col items-center gap-2 rounded-xl border border-border-subtle bg-surface-card px-4 py-5 text-center">\n      <button\n        type="button"\n        aria-label={Remove ${player.firstName} ${player.lastName} from the comparison}\n        className="absolute top-2 left-2 rounded-md px-1.5 text-base leading-none text-text-muted transition-colors hover:text-text-primary focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-brand-accent"\n        onClick={onRemove}\n      >\n        ✕\n      \n      <PlayerHeadshot player={player} size="lg" />\n      <div className="text-base font-semibold tracking-tight text-text-primary">\n        {player.firstName} {player.lastName}\n      \n      <div className="flex items-center justify-center gap-2 text-xs text-text-secondary">\n        {player.team && <TeamBadge team={player.team} size="sm" />}\n        {player.team?.name ?? "Free agent"}\n      \n      <div className="text-[11px] font-medium tracking-[0.16em] text-text-muted uppercase">\n        {player.position}\n      \n    \n  );\n}\n\n// An unfilled column: the comparison always shows at least two slots, so the\n// page reads as "two players go here" before anything is picked rather than\n// as an empty page with a single button.\nfunction EmptySlotTile({\n  slotNumber,\n  onSelect,\n  excludedPlayerIds,\n}: {\n  slotNumber: number;\n  onSelect: (player: Player) => void;\n  excludedPlayerIds: string[];\n}) {\n  return (\n    <div className="flex flex-col items-center gap-3 rounded-xl border border-dashed border-border-subtle bg-surface-card/40 px-4 py-5">\n      \n      <PlayerSearchCombobox\n        onSelect={onSelect}\n        excludedPlayerIds={excludedPlayerIds}\n        placeholder="Select player"\n        label={Select player ${slotNumber}}\n      />\n    \n  );\n}\n\nfunction LoadingSlotTile() {\n  return <Skeleton className="h-[186px] rounded-xl" />;\n}\n\nexport function ComparePage() {\n  const [playerIds, setPlayerIds] = useSelectedPlayerIds();\n  // Extra empty columns the user asked for with "Add another player", beyond\n  // the two the comparison always shows. Consumed as they get filled.\n  const [extraSlots, setExtraSlots] = useState(0);\n\n  const comparisonQuery = useQuery({\n    queryKey: ["playerComparison", playerIds],\n    queryFn: () => fetchPlayerComparison(playerIds),\n    enabled: playerIds.length >= MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_COMPARISON,\n  });\n\n  // The compare endpoint needs two players; a lone selected player (just\n  // added, or left after a removal) is resolved on its own so the tile still\n  // renders while the user picks an opponent.\n  const lonePlayerId = playerIds.length === 1 ? playerIds[0] : undefined;\n  const lonePlayerQuery = useQuery({\n    queryKey: ["playerComparison", "lone", lonePlayerId],\n    queryFn: async (): Promise => {\n      const [player, stats] = await Promise.all([\n        fetchPlayer(lonePlayerId!),\n        fetchPlayerStats(lonePlayerId!),\n      ]);\n      return { player, seasonAverages: stats.seasonAverages };\n    },\n    enabled: !!lonePlayerId,\n  });\n\n  const isComparing = playerIds.length >= MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_COMPARISON;\n  const entries: PlayerComparisonEntry[] = isComparing\n    ? (comparisonQuery.data?.players ?? [])\n    : lonePlayerQuery.data\n      ? [lonePlayerQuery.data]\n      : [];\n\n  const activeQuery = isComparing ? comparisonQuery : lonePlayerQuery;\n  // A selection that hasn't resolved yet still owns its column, so the layout\n  // doesn't jump once the data lands.\n  const claimedColumns = Math.max(entries.length, playerIds.length);\n  const slotCount = Math.min(\n    Math.max(claimedColumns, MIN_PLAYERS_FOR_COMPARISON) + extraSlots,\n    MAX_PLAYERS\n  );\n  const slots = Array.from({ length: slotCount }, (, index) => entries[index] ?? null);\n  const canAddSlot = slotCount < MAX_PLAYERS;\n  const columns = gridStyle(slotCount);\n\n  function addPlayer(player: Player) {\n    if (playerIds.includes(player.id) || playerIds.length >= MAX_PLAYERS) return;\n    setPlayerIds([...playerIds, player.id]);\n    setExtraSlots((slots) => Math.max(0, slots - 1));\n  }\n\n  function removePlayer(playerId: string) {\n    setPlayerIds(playerIds.filter((id) => id !== playerId));\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div className="mx-auto max-w-6xl p-6">\n      <h1 className="text-center text-lg font-semibold tracking-tight text-text-primary">\n        Player comparison\n      \n      <p className="mt-1 mb-6 text-center text-xs text-text-muted">\n        Compare up to {MAX_PLAYERS} players side by side on their season averages. Age arrives once\n        player bios land.\n      \n\n      <div className="grid items-center gap-x-3" style={columns}>\n        <div className="pr-3">\n          {canAddSlot && (\n            <button\n              type="button"\n              className="flex w-full flex-col items-center gap-2 rounded-lg px-2 py-4 text-center transition-colors hover:bg-surface-card focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-brand-accent"\n              onClick={() => setExtraSlots((slots) => slots + 1)}\n            >\n              <span className="flex size-9 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-brand-accent text-lg leading-none text-brand-accent">\n                +\n              \n              <span className="text-xs font-medium text-brand-accent">Add another player\n            \n          )}\n        \n\n        {slots.map((entry, index) =>\n          entry ? (\n            <PlayerTile\n              key={entry.player.id}\n              player={entry.player}\n              onRemove={() => removePlayer(entry.player.id)}\n            />\n          ) : index < playerIds.length && activeQuery.isPending ? (\n                      <div\n                        key={entry.player.id}\n                        className="rounded-lg bg-surface-card px-3 py-2 text-center"\n                      >\n                        <span\n                          className={cn(\n                            "inline-block rounded-full px-2.5 py-0.5 text-sm text-text-primary tabular-nums",\n                            bestIndexes.has(index) &&\n                              "bg-brand-accent/15 font-semibold text-brand-accent"\n                          )}\n                        >\n                          {row.render(entry.seasonAverages, entry.player)}\n                        \n                      \n                    ) : (\n                      // Keeps an unfilled slot's column width so the filled\n                      // columns stay aligned with their tiles above.\n                      <div key={empty-${index}} aria-hidden="true" />\n                    )\n                  )}\n                \n              );\n            })}\n          \n        ))}\n    \n  );\n}\n" }, "caller": { "type": "direct" } } ]

2026/08/29, 22:12:36
Both changes are in.
Headings now centre on the player columns. Each group is a single grid (same columns template as the header), and the heading block is placed with gridColumn: "2 / -1" — it spans only the player columns, so with two tiles it lands exactly on the gap between them instead of drifting left of centre from including the label column. The caption ("made / attempted (accuracy)") rides in the same spanning block.
The page now always shows at least two slots, matching screenshots 2 and 3:
Selected	Renders
0	two dashed placeholder tiles, each with a placeholder avatar + "Select player" search
1	the real tile + one placeholder tile
2+	real tiles only; "Add another player" adds a further empty slot, up to 4

"Add another player" no longer opens a search in the left column — it appends an empty slot, and you pick the player in that slot's own "Select player" box. Filling a slot consumes the extra one, so the column count stays stable.
Two details worth noting:
    • A selected-but-not-yet-loaded slot renders a skeleton tile rather than a "Select player" box, so the layout doesn't flash a placeholder mid-load and doesn't jump when data lands.
    • Stat rows render an invisible spacer cell for any unfilled slot, so filled columns stay aligned with their tiles above even when a placeholder is present.
    • PlayerSearchCombobox gained a label prop — several are on screen at once, so each gets a distinct accessible name ("Select player 1", "Select player 2").
I dropped the "Add at least one more player to see the comparison." sentence; the placeholder tile carries that meaning now.
Verified: tsc -b clean, oxlint clean on the new files (three pre-existing fast-refresh warnings elsewhere are untouched), full web suite 110 passed. Tests updated to cover the two-empty-slots state, the one-real-plus-one-empty state, and "Add another player" adding a third slot.
API still needs npm run db:up:test && npm run test --prefix apps/api on your machine — Docker isn't reachable here. Nothing committed yet.

Before the Lottie question — you already have a cheaper option installed.
First: you may not need Lottie
lucide-react is already a dependency and already in use (HomePage.tsx:3 imports ArrowRight). A Plus / UserPlus / CirclePlus from it would fix the "looks off" problem immediately:
    • inherits currentColor, so it picks up text-brand-accent and stays correct if the theme changes
    • crisp at any size, no raster blur
    • zero new dependencies, zero bundle cost beyond the one icon
    • no attribution required (lucide is ISC; credit isn't a UI obligation)
The current + is a literal text character in a bordered circle, which is why it reads as slightly off — the glyph's optical weight and centring don't match a drawn icon. Swapping in <Plus /> is a two-line change.
If you specifically want motion, a CSS transition on hover (scale/rotate the plus) gets you most of the effect for nothing.
If you do go Lottie: pick JSON
	JSON (Lottie)	APNG
Type	Vector	Raster
Scaling	Any size, one file	Fixed; need 2–3× for retina
Colour	Baked in, but editable	Baked in, won't follow your theme
Playback control	Play on hover / once on click / pause	Loops, essentially uncontrollable
File size	Usually a few KB	Much larger
Needs a library	Yes	No — plain <img>

JSON, clearly — except for one caveat that matters here: the colour is baked into the file. An icon exported in blue will stay blue on your orange/brown theme unless you hand-edit the colour arrays in the JSON.
Real costs to weigh before committing:
    • Bundle: lottie-web is ~250 KB minified (~70 KB gzipped) for one button. That's a lot of weight for one +.
    • Tests: jsdom doesn't run canvas/SVG animation, so you'd need to mock the player in ComparePage.spec.tsx. That's actual work on tests that currently pass clean.
    • Self-host it. Download the file into apps/web/src/assets/ and import it — don't hotlink the LottieFiles CDN.
APNG is only the right call if you're not willing to add a library at all.
Size
    • JSON — irrelevant, it's vector. If the download dialog forces a choice, pick whatever matches your render size; it scales regardless.
    • APNG — your button circle is size-9 (36 px), so download 72 px (2×) or 108 px (3×) and render it at className="size-9".
Attribution
Check the licence on that specific asset's download page — don't assume. It varies by creator even on the same site:
    • LottieFiles — per-creator. Many are the Lottie Simple License (no attribution needed); plenty are CC BY 4.0, which requires credit.
    • Lordicon free tier — attribution link back to lordicon.com is mandatory.
    • IconScout free — usually requires attribution.
If credit is required, this repo already has a pattern to follow, from the Unsplash hero photo:
    1. A comment at the asset constant explaining the licence and why it's OK to use — HomePage.tsx:6-11.
    2. A visible credit link where the licence demands it — HomePage renders Photo: Logan Weaver / Unsplash as a small corner link (HomePage.tsx:76-83). For a UI icon, a footer credit is more appropriate than one stuck next to the button.
    3. Add it to the tech stack list — PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md:56 already lists "lucide-react — icons"; the Lottie library and the icon's source belong there too.
One gap worth noting: docs/ai-usage.md is the AI attribution ledger, not an asset one, and there's no third-party-assets section anywhere yet. If you're adding a licensed asset, that's the moment to start a short "Third-party assets" section in the README — one line per asset, with source, licence, and link.
Want me to swap the button to a lucide Plus (with a subtle hover transition) so you can see whether that's enough before taking on the Lottie dependency?

It's at ComparePage.tsx:349-351:
<span className="flex size-9 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-brand-accent text-lg leading-none text-brand-accent">
  +
</span>
Why it sits low
items-center centres the line box, not the visible glyph. A text + is positioned relative to the baseline, and fonts reserve asymmetric space around it — roughly 0.8em above the baseline for ascenders, 0.2em below for descenders. The plus glyph's own centre (the "math axis") sits only about 0.25em above the baseline, so once the line box is centred, the visible plus lands a bit below the circle's geometric centre. leading-none reduces the effect but can't remove it, because the baseline position inside the em box is fixed by the font.
That's also why it'll drift if the font ever changes — it's not a value you can tune once and trust.
Three ways to fix it
1. Real fix — use an SVG icon. lucide-react is already installed, and its icons are drawn on a geometric grid with no baseline involved, so they centre exactly:
import { Plus } from "lucide-react";

<span className="flex size-9 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-brand-accent text-brand-accent">
  <Plus className="size-4" strokeWidth={2.5} aria-hidden="true" />
</span>
Drop text-lg leading-none — they're only there to manage the text glyph.
2. No new import — draw the plus with two spans. Also geometric, no font dependency:
<span className="relative flex size-9 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-brand-accent">
  <span className="absolute h-[1.5px] w-3.5 rounded-full bg-brand-accent" />
  <span className="absolute h-3.5 w-[1.5px] rounded-full bg-brand-accent" />
</span>
3. Quick nudge if you want to keep the text glyph. Add a bottom-padding of one pixel to push the line box up:
className="flex size-9 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-brand-accent pb-px text-lg leading-none text-brand-accent"
Tune with pb-px → pb-0.5 until it looks right. This is the least robust option — it's calibrated to whatever font is loading right now.
I'd take option 1. Want me to apply it? It's a two-line change and the tests target the button by its accessible name, so nothing breaks.

2026/08/29, 22:57:39
All four changes are in.
Cancel button on the empty tile. Only on slots you opened with "Add another player" — the two slots a comparison always shows can't be cancelled away, so you can never reduce the page below a usable state. Clicking it decrements the extra-slot count.
Empty rows show a dash. An unclaimed column now renders a real cell (—, muted, on a lighter card fill) for every stat row instead of an invisible spacer, so the column reads as "waiting for a player" rather than as a hole.
No field appears twice. Position now lives on the tile only (removed from General); team now lives in General only (removed from the tile), rendered with its TeamBadge beside the name.
Positions spelled out via a small dict — G → Guard, F → Forward, C → Center, split on - so G-F → Guard-Forward and F-C → Forward-Center. Anything unrecognised passes through untouched, which matters because the roster feed emits "N/A" for players it has no position for (rosters.py:33) — that renders as N/A rather than being mangled.
Age is now the first General row — but I have to flag this: it renders — for everyone. Player.birthDate doesn't exist on this branch (it's on the unmerged bio branch), so there's genuinely nothing to compute from. I added the row anyway so it lights up automatically when bios merge, and the page subheading already says "Age arrives once player bios land" — but if a row of dashes bothers you before then, say so and I'll pull it until the merge.
Verified: tsc -b clean, oxlint clean on the changed files, full web suite 112 passed (added tests for cancel-slot, position spelling with G-F, position absent from General, and dashes filling an unclaimed column).

Yes — PR straight to main. Your branch was cut from main, so there's nothing stacked to unwind. That's also what GIT_METHODOLOGY.md prescribes: branch off main, PR <branch> -> main, merge once everyone has reviewed.
Here's the actual state:
    • main = 72e0462
    • player-bio-fields = main + 3 commits, a clean fast-forward, no divergence
    • player-comparisons = same base, and your work is still uncommitted
What to expect when bio lands
Once bio merges, main moves 3 commits ahead of you, and you bring it in. I checked the file overlap — exactly two files will need attention:
File	Bio adds	Yours adds
PlayerProfilePage.tsx	Bio card, age helpers, isBioLoaded	MPG tile, FTA/G tile, "Compare" link
types/nba.ts	fields on Player	fields on SeasonAverages, PlayerComparisonEntry

types/nba.ts will probably auto-merge (different interfaces). PlayerProfilePage.tsx likely won't — you both edit the header block and the stat-tile grid. It's a small, mechanical conflict.
Everything else is disjoint. Notably no Prisma conflict at all — your branch touches zero schema/migration files.
Suggested order
    1. Commit and push now, open the PR to main immediately. Don't wait for bio — the two are independent, and an open PR lets review start in parallel.
    2. When bio merges, git fetch && git merge origin/main and resolve those two files. Use merge rather than rebase once the branch is pushed and under review, so you don't rewrite history teammates have pulled. (Right now, while it's unpushed, rebase would be fine.)
    3. Then Age becomes a one-liner. The row already exists in STAT_GROUPS; it just swaps its render from the placeholder dash to reading player.birthDate. Fold it into this PR if bio lands before yours is approved; otherwise ship as-is and follow up.
    4. Re-run the migration drift check after the merge — steps 1–3 of the plan from earlier. Not because you added a migration, but because your branch will then contain bio's, and you want to confirm it's already applied to Supabase.
One thing worth knowing: the bio branch's tip commit is dbf7bbd chore: run prisma migrate deploy on api start. That's the fix for the gap that caused the #47 revert — nothing was applying migrations on deploy. So bio merging closes that hole for everyone, which is a good argument for pushing your teammates to finish that review.
Want me to stage and propose the commits now? I'd split them: API endpoint + derived fields, the compare page, then docs.

Here's the runbook. I've already scanned the pending changes for secrets — clean, and no .env files are pending. You should still eyeball git diff --staged before each commit, per your own convention.
Step 0 — Pre-flight
# Web: already verified green here
cd apps/web && npx tsc -b && npm run lint && npm test

# API: NOT yet verified — needs Docker, which I couldn't reach
cd ../.. && npm run db:up:test
cd apps/api && npx prisma generate && npx tsc --noEmit && npx eslint src && npm test
The API suite is the one gap in what I've verified. Don't commit until it passes.
Step 1 — Four commits on player-comparisons
Commit 1 — API
git add apps/api/src/players/stats.service.ts \
        apps/api/src/players/stats.service.spec.ts \
        apps/api/src/players/players.controller.ts \
        apps/api/test/players.e2e-spec.ts
feat: add per-game season averages and a player compare endpoint
Commit 2 — the compare page
git add apps/web/src/pages/ComparePage.tsx \
        apps/web/src/pages/ComparePage.spec.tsx \
        apps/web/src/components/PlayerSearchCombobox.tsx \
        apps/web/src/lib/nbaApi.ts \
        apps/web/src/lib/nbaApi.spec.ts \
        apps/web/src/types/nba.ts \
        apps/web/src/App.tsx \
        apps/web/src/components/landing/LandingHeader.tsx
feat: add player comparison page
Commit 3 — profile page (separate because it also carries the entry point into commit 2's page, so it has to land after it)
git add apps/web/src/pages/PlayerProfilePage.tsx
feat: show minutes, free throw attempts and a compare link on player profiles
Commit 4 — docs
git add docs/plans/player-comparisons.md docs/PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md
docs: document the player comparisons feature and its data audit
⚠️ Use that exact path — not git add docs/plans/. That directory is untracked as a whole and contains two files unrelated to this feature (see Step 3).
Step 2 — Push and open the PR
git push -u origin player-comparisons
Open player-comparisons -> main on Gitea. Worth putting in the description:
    • Age renders as a dash until player-bio-fields merges; the row is already in place and needs only its render wired to birthDate.
    • Offensive/defensive rebound split is deferred — needs a schema migration and a re-ingest, documented in the plan doc.
    • No schema or migration changes in this PR (git diff --stat main...HEAD -- apps/api/prisma/ is empty), so there's nothing to deploy ahead of it.
Don't wait for bio to open this — the two are independent and review can run in parallel.
Step 3 — The two unrelated docs, on their own branch
docs/plans/ also holds shot-charts-ballr-investigation.md and player-similarity-investigation.md. They're unrelated to comparisons, so bundling them breaks your "don't bundle unrelated changes" rule. Do this after Step 1 (untracked files survive branch switches, so they'll still be sitting there):
git checkout -b feature-investigations main
git add docs/plans/shot-charts-ballr-investigation.md \
        docs/plans/player-similarity-investigation.md
git commit -m "docs: add shot chart and player similarity feasibility investigations"
git push -u origin feature-investigations
git checkout player-comparisons
Expect those two files to vanish from your working tree on that last checkout — that's correct, they're committed safely on the other branch.
Step 4 — After bio merges to main
git checkout player-comparisons
git fetch origin
git merge origin/main          # merge, not rebase — the branch is pushed and under review
Resolve the two overlapping files (PlayerProfilePage.tsx, types/nba.ts), then:
cd apps/api && npx prisma generate
docker exec -i nba-analytics-postgres psql -U postgres -c "DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS prisma_shadow; CREATE DATABASE prisma_shadow;"
npx prisma migrate diff --from-migrations ./prisma/migrations \
  --to-schema-datamodel ./prisma/schema.prisma \
  --shadow-database-url "postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:55432/prisma_shadow" --exit-code
Exit 0 confirms bio's migration is intact through your merge. Then wire the Age row and re-run both suites.
Step 5 — Merge
Only once every teammate has reviewed and agreed, per GIT_METHODOLOGY.md. Commit 3 depends on commit 2, so keep the commits in order if anyone suggests reordering or cherry-picking.

Yes, that's right — 4 is correct on this branch.
The 5th one, 20260820103112_player_bio_fields, exists only on player-bio-fields. It hasn't merged to main yet, and your branch was cut from main, so it isn't here:
Branch	Migrations
main	4
player-comparisons (yours)	4
player-bio-fields	5 — the four above plus 20260820103112_player_bio_fields

This is exactly why Age shows a dash right now: the migration that adds birthDate to Player lives on that unmerged branch, so neither your schema nor main's has the column.
You'll get the 5th one automatically at Step 4, when you git merge origin/main after bio lands. That's also precisely what the drift check in Step 4 verifies — that bio's migration came through your merge intact and still matches schema.prisma.
One thing to watch when you do that merge: if a teammate ever regenerates that migration (say the bio PR gets reworked), its timestamp prefix changes, and you'd end up with two migrations doing the same thing. If Step 4's migrate diff exits non-zero, that's the first place to look.

No, that one's harmless. Here's the evidence from your repo:
i/lf    w/lf    apps/api/src/players/players.controller.ts
i/lf    w/crlf  apps/api/src/players/stats.service.ts
i/lf    w/crlf  apps/web/src/App.tsx
i/ is what's stored in git, w/ is your working copy. Every file is i/lf — 0 files are stored with CRLF. You have core.autocrlf=true (the Git for Windows default), which means git normalizes to LF when committing and expands to CRLF when checking out. The repo content is LF and stays LF.
The warning fired on players.controller.ts specifically because I wrote it with LF endings, unlike the CRLF working copies around it. Git is just saying "next time I check this out, it'll come back as CRLF." Nothing to fix, and it won't show up as a diff.
The one thing worth knowing
Your repo has no .gitattributes, so line-ending behaviour depends on each teammate's personal git config. Nothing is broken today — the index is 100% LF — but if a teammate has core.autocrlf=false on Windows, they could commit CRLF blobs, and you'd see a one-line change render as a whole-file diff in review. That's the failure mode this warning is a distant cousin of.
Cheap insurance, as its own chore: commit on a separate branch — not in the comparisons PR:
# .gitattributes
* text=auto eol=lf
Because your index is already all-LF, this produces no content diff — it only makes the behaviour explicit and stops future working copies from drifting. Teammates would see their working files switch to LF on next checkout, which their editors won't care about.
Up to you whether that's worth raising with the team. It doesn't block anything you're doing now — carry on with Step 0.

Facts checked: 14 files, +1047/−7, and zero files under apps/api/prisma/ — worth stating explicitly given the #47 incident. There's no PR template in the repo, so this is free-form.
PR title
feat: add player comparison page and compare endpoint
PR body
Adds a `/compare` page that puts 2–4 players side by side on their season
averages, plus the endpoint that feeds it.

## What's in it

**API**
- `GET /v1/players/compare?ids=a,b,c` — returns each player with their derived
  season line in one request instead of N. De-duplicates ids, preserves order,
  400s outside 2–4 players, 404s on an unknown id.
- `DerivedSeasonAverages` gains `minutesPerGame` and made/attempted-per-game
  for FG, 3P and FT. All derived at request time from existing
  `PlayerGameStat` rows — no new data, no ingestion change.

**Web**
- New public route `/compare`, plus a `Compare` nav entry and a "Compare" link
  on the player profile.
- Selection lives in `?ids=`, so a comparison is shareable and survives reload.
- Player tiles with add-by-search / remove; the page always shows at least two
  slots so it reads as "two players go here" before anything is picked.
- Stats grouped under headings that carry the `(per game)` qualifier, so row
  names stay short. Leader in each row is highlighted.
- Profile page also picks up the new MPG and FTA/G tiles.

## No schema or migration changes

`git diff --name-only main...HEAD -- apps/api/prisma/` is empty. Nothing needs
to be applied to Supabase before or after this deploys.

## Deliberately not included

**Age** — needs `Player.birthDate`, which only exists on the unmerged
`player-bio-fields` branch. The Age row is already in place rendering a dash,
so it lights up with a one-line change once that merges. The page subheading
says so.

**Offensive / defensive rebound split** — `PlayerGameStat` stores only
`rebounds`. `BoxScoreTraditionalV3` returns `reboundsOffensive` and
`reboundsDefensive` in the same response we already fetch (no extra API call),
but wiring them up needs a schema migration, a `games.py` change, a re-ingest
to backfill existing rows, and seed fixtures. That's its own change — this PR
shows total rebounds.

Everything else originally asked for is present: height, team, games played,
minutes per game, points, FT attempts + %, 3P%, FG%, rebounds, assists,
steals, turnovers, blocks.

## Worth a reviewer's attention

- **MPG reads slightly low.** `games.py`'s `_parse_minutes_to_int` floors
  minutes to whole numbers at ingestion, so per-game minutes inherit that.
  Pre-existing, not introduced here, but it's visible for the first time.
- **The compare route is declared before `:id`** in the controller, so
  `/v1/players/compare` isn't swallowed as a player id. Please don't reorder.
- **Shooting rows highlight the higher makes per game, not the better
  percentage.** They render as `made / attempted (accuracy)` and sit under
  "Points", so volume is the intended comparison. Happy to flip it if the team
  disagrees.

## Verification

- [x] `apps/web` — `tsc -b`, `oxlint`, 112 tests passing
- [ ] `apps/api` — `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, `npm test` against the disposable
      Postgres
Fill in that last checkbox yourself — I couldn't reach Docker, so the API suite is the one thing unverified.

After player-bio-fields merges
1. Bring main in — merge, not rebase, since this branch is already pushed.
git checkout player-comparisons
git fetch origin
git merge origin/main
2. Resolve the two overlapping files. Nothing else conflicts.
    • apps/web/src/pages/PlayerProfilePage.tsx — bio adds the Bio card, calculateAge, isBioLoaded; yours adds the MPG/FTA tiles and the Compare link. Keep both.
    • apps/web/src/types/nba.ts — bio extends Player, yours extends SeasonAverages. Likely auto-merges.
3. Regenerate the Prisma client, or tsc fails on the new birthDate field:
cd apps/api && npx prisma generate
4. Verify bio's migration survived the merge — this is the #47 guard:
docker exec -i nba-analytics-postgres psql -U postgres \
  -c "DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS prisma_shadow; CREATE DATABASE prisma_shadow;"
npx prisma migrate diff --from-migrations ./prisma/migrations \
  --to-schema-datamodel ./prisma/schema.prisma \
  --shadow-database-url "postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:55432/prisma_shadow" \
  --exit-code
Exit 0 means the five migrations fully describe the schema. Non-zero usually means the bio migration got regenerated with a new timestamp during review and you now have two doing the same thing.
5. Wire the Age row. In ComparePage.tsx's STAT_GROUPS, the General group's first row currently reads { label: "Age", render: () => NO_VALUE }. Point it at birthDate.
The bio branch already has a calculateAge helper inside PlayerProfilePage.tsx — extract it to a shared module rather than copying it, so the profile and the comparison can't drift apart on how age is computed.
6. Drop the "Age arrives once player bios land" clause from the page subheading — easy to forget, and it'll be wrong.
7. Update the Age tests. ComparePage.spec.tsx has a test asserting exactly two dashes for the two players' Age cells. It will fail once Age resolves — that's the test doing its job. Update it to assert a real age, and add birthDate to the spec's makePlayer fixture.
8. Re-run both suites, push, and note in the PR that the bio dependency is now resolved so reviewers re-check that section.
